The assignee's `plan` session then breaks it down, and a `plan` session of the epic's assignee assigns each of its tasks once ready (added in 0.8).
4. It is done when its tasks are. An epic moves to `verifying` when every task under it is `accepted` or `cancelled` and at least one is accepted; its own exit criteria are then run by its reviewer, the Product Manager when the Scrum Master broke it down and the human when the Product Manager did, since a reviewer is never the assignee (5.1); and its acceptance requires the user, like its approval did. The epic's assignee closes it out in a `plan` session once its tasks are done, writing its completion note and asking for `verifying`, or filing more tasks when the user's message asks for them; when the user reviews it, Catervas runs its mechanical criteria for them first (5.4; added in 0.8), and so it does for the Product Manager, who reviews the epic from its contract, Catervas's results, and each task's completion note (5.4; added in 0.10). A rejected epic goes back to `in_progress` by the governor's row, as a task does, and its assignee's close-out `plan` session, once every task under it is done, is given the last rejection, the failed criteria's ids and the review note, and asked to file the tasks that fix it rather than close it out again; once a task has been created under the epic since that rejection, the close-out is the plain one. A new rejection raises the iteration, which the iteration limit bounds (added in 0.10).

A request Catervas triages itself (added in 0.72; ADR 0042). The request for a plan's new version with a raised marketing budget (6.5) is filed by Catervas for the owner's decision, not from a person's words: `task.created` carries `raises: <plan>` (8.5) and Catervas records `request.triaged { size: small, reason: "a raised marketing budget for <plan>", triaged_by: "catervas" }` in the same call, so no triage session runs and no window to overrule remains; it is refined and approved as any request is, and it skips the sprint queue (3).

A user who wants to write the epic themselves does so in the editor or as a file, locks it (5.11), and approves it; the breakdown still happens by the assignee. When the triage sized a request as small and the Product Manager finds while refining that it is not, it re-triages the request itself as large (`catervas_triage_request` accepts this one change from the Product Manager of a `refining` standalone task); the standalone task becomes the epic and its refining starts over. The user may overrule any triage until refining starts.

The product roadmap, `.catervas/product/roadmap.md`, and the product documents under `.catervas/product/` are written by the Product Manager only through `catervas_write_product_doc`, only for approved epics, and every write is a `product_doc.written` event, so that the user can see what changed in the roadmap and why.

## 6. Launch roles

Each role ships as a directory under `roles/<role>/` with `role.yaml` (mandate, what the role produces, what it may not do, its default model and effort, and the skills it ships with), `system.md` (the prompt), and a `skills/` folder holding one `<skill-name>/SKILL.md` per skill in the Agent Skills format. A role's permission tiers and session limits are not in `role.yaml`: they are the governor's defaults (5.6, 5.5), which the team file overrides per agent (`grants`, `revokes`, `budgets.session`), and an agent's own `model` in the team file overrides the role's default model and effort. A copy in the role file that the governor did not read would be one a user edited expecting an effect (added in 0.8). The user can edit all of it. A role's skills are written into the prompt of every session of the role (ADR 0011). The user can also give one agent, or the whole team, a skill of their own (added in 0.40; 6.7, ADR 0034): the team's lives in `.catervas/skills/<name>/` and one agent's in `.catervas/agents/<agent_id>/skills/<name>/`, and a name resolves agent, then team, then role, a team or agent skill replacing a shipped skill of its name only when the person said so.

### 6.1 Product Manager

Mandate: own the backlog, translate every request from the user into a contract, an epic or a standalone task as the triage sized it, after asking the user its questions (5.16), accept work against contracts, keep the product pointed at a user need. The PM is the source of exit criteria and the last non-human gate. When the team has no Scrum Master, the PM also triages requests, breaks approved epics into tasks, and assigns them.

Produces: epic contracts and standalone task contracts, the questions it asks the user, product decisions, the product roadmap and requirements under `.catervas/product/` (only for approved epics), release scope.

Cannot: write application code, which only the Software Developer and the UI/UX Designer do (changed in 0.31), and the DevOps Engineer in an incident's fix (6.9; changed in 0.32); work a task outside the team's `document_paths` (5.3; added in 0.11); write product documents for an epic the user has not approved; run the test suite as a reviewer of its own contracts; accept a task without a reviewer's verification event.

Default tools: read, network (for competitor and docs research), the task and decision tools. The decision tools are `catervas_write_decision`, which the Product Manager and the Architect alone may call, and `catervas_read_decisions`, which every agent may (5.8; added in 0.16). Default model: the strongest available, since contract quality is leverage on everything downstream.

Kit (added in 0.46; 6.7): five skills, `asking-the-right-questions`, `writing-requirements`, `prioritising-the-backlog`, `scoping-a-release` and `using-product-sources`, and four services: Amplitude, Linear and Notion, which it only reads, and GitHub, which it reads for issues and an organisation's project boards and writes to, an issue or a comment, only after the human allows that call (changed in 0.65; ADR 0044).

Data pipelines (added in 0.76; phase 7 step 10e). The Product Manager decides the Procurement Specialist's requests for a data source (6.10): in a session about no task it approves one that is free and sends no data out, declines one that is not needed, and escalates the rest to the owner. It cannot approve one that costs money, whose cost is not known or that sends the project's data out, and it never decides a request it did not receive a session for.

### 6.2 Scrum Master

Mandate: keep work flowing and keep the human informed. Triages every request from the user as large or small (5.16), in a session of its own on the cheaper model whatever its own, given the triage tool alone (added in 0.10). Breaks approved epics into tasks with clear deliverables and exit criteria and assigns them to the appropriate agents. Runs planning, standup, review, and retro, one session each, and the Product Manager runs them when the team has no active Scrum Master (5.9; changed in 0.15). Plans each sprint the human starts, once, from the ready backlog, the Backlog when the team plans its work in sprints (section 3; changed in 0.36), and within the sprint's budget, in the planning ceremony, where it also posts the escalation digest (section 3, 5.5, 5.7; added in 0.12, changed in 0.15). Appends what the retro learned to `team/retro.md` (5.8; added in 0.15). Enforces WIP limits (default: one unfinished task per agent, counted as 5.2 says: every task the agent holds that is not `accepted` or `cancelled`). Judges each contract against the Definition of Ready's judgment criteria once every other rule passes, in a session given `catervas_record_judgment` alone (5.3; changed in 0.10). Is assigned the approved epics, which the Product Manager then reviews (5.16; added in 0.10). Owns escalation hygiene.

Produces: triage decisions, task contracts under epics, sprint plans, standup summaries, retro notes, escalation digests.

Cannot: change an epic's requirements or contract; change a frozen contract; write application code, which only the Software Developer and the UI/UX Designer do (changed in 0.31), and the DevOps Engineer in an incident's fix (6.9; changed in 0.32); work a task outside the team's `document_paths` (5.3); accept work (changed in 0.11).

Default tools: read, the board tools, the triage tool, the judgment tool (added in 0.10), the sprint planning tool (added in 0.12), the retro tool (added in 0.15), and the contract tools for the tasks of an epic it is breaking down. Runs on a mid-tier model; its work is coordination, not deep reasoning.

Kit (added in 0.46; 6.7): three skills, `planning-a-sprint`, `running-ceremonies` and `writing-escalation-digests`, and no service. The chat bridge is planned in the last phase.

### 6.3 Architect

Mandate: hold the shape of the system. Writes architecture decision records, sets constraints that go into contracts (allowed paths, patterns to follow), and reviews the developer's diffs for design, writing the review note. An Architect's task is a document: it runs no spikes, and it does not write application code, which only the Developer and the UI/UX Designer do (changed in 0.10, and in 0.31 for the Designer, from the founder's decision of 2026-09-24). Its tasks are held to the team's `document_paths` and work on `docs/CTV-<n>` (5.3, 5.14; added in 0.11).

Produces: ADRs, design notes attached to contracts, review notes.

Cannot: write application code, merge or push a shared branch, accept work (changed in 0.10).

Default tools: read, write_workspace, execute, network, git_local, so that it commits the documents that are its work (changed in 0.10). It records its decisions under `.catervas/decisions/` with `catervas_write_decision`, which the Product Manager and the Architect alone may call, and reads them with `catervas_read_decisions` (5.8; added in 0.16).

Kit (added in 0.47; 6.7): five skills, `designing-apis-and-data`, `reviewing-dependencies`, `security-review`, `setting-performance-budgets` and `using-architecture-sources`, and four services it only reads: Context7 (library documentation), Grep (public code search), OSV (known flaws in packages) and GitHub (the code and pull requests of the user's own repositories, private ones included, reached with a key the user pastes until Catervas Cloud runs (phase 8) and by a sign-in through Catervas Cloud with Catervas's GitHub App from then; changed in 0.65, ADR 0044).

### 6.4 Software Developer

Mandate: implement contracts. With the UI/UX Designer, the only role that writes application code, beside the DevOps Engineer's incident fixes (6.9) (added in 0.11; changed in 0.31 and 0.32). Works on a task branch, `feature/CTV-<n>` or `fix/CTV-<n>` by the contract's `change` (5.14; changed in 0.11), runs the exit criteria before declaring done, writes a completion note.

Produces: diffs, commits on task branches, completion notes.

Cannot: modify contracts, accept work, push to shared branches without the `git_remote` grant, touch files outside allowed paths.

Default tools: read, write_workspace, execute, git_local. The developer runs the project's own tooling; Catervas does not reimplement a coding agent. See section 8.

Kit (added in 0.48; 6.7): six skills, `test-driven-development`, `debugging`, `safe-migrations`, `testing-per-stack`, `answering-a-review` and `using-docs-and-the-browser`, and one service it only reads, Context7 (library documentation), the Architect's entry with its own `why`, signed in to by route 1. Its browser is the built-in Playwright of the UI/UX Designer's kit (6.7), off unless the user turns it on for the Developer on its page; it opens the task's preview and nothing else, in the Developer's `explore` and `implement` sessions, and a Developer's `verify` session is not given it. The Developer has no `network` tier; Context7 is its one outbound call, its `query` and `libraryName` are words the agent writes, sent to Context7 (Upstash), each call recorded as `tool.called`, and they are not tagged `external_effect`, so no documentation lookup waits for approval. A development database and deploy status are not in this kit: the database a task uses runs in the sandbox or the preview, which a host server cannot reach, and deploy status is the DevOps Engineer's (6.9).

### 6.5 Marketing Specialist

Mandate: own the business's marketing (changed in 0.52 on the founder's decision of 2026-10-05, ADR 0042; `docs/design/marketing-specialist.md`; built in phase 7 steps 08b to 08f). It owns four things: the brand kit, the brand persona, the marketing plan, and the social presence on the business's channels. It researches the market, the audience, competitors, the words customers search for and what each channel costs, to decide the best plan; writes the plan with its budget for the owner to approve; posts on the business's social channels and runs its search ads as the approved plan says; and still writes release notes, landing and README copy and positioning, and synthesizes user feedback the user pastes in (the research and the plan added in 0.10).

Produces: files under the team's `document_paths` (default `docs/**`, `**/*.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md`; changed in 0.11), among them the brand kit (`docs/marketing/brand/brand-kit.md` and its files under `docs/marketing/brand/assets/`), the brand persona (`docs/marketing/brand/persona.md`), each marketing plan (`docs/marketing/plans/MP-<n>.md`) and its research (`docs/marketing/research/`); positioning decisions; the posts Catervas sends for it; the Google Ads campaigns it runs for an approved plan. While the team has an active Marketing Specialist, another role's task may not name `docs/marketing/**` in its allowed paths (5.3; added in 0.52); every agent may read them, and the UI/UX Designer takes the project's colours and voice from the brand kit.

Cannot: change application code, which only the Software Developer and the UI/UX Designer do (changed in 0.31), and the DevOps Engineer in an incident's fix (6.9; changed in 0.32); work a task outside the team's `document_paths` (5.3); publish or send without the human allowing that call, unless the owner's approved marketing plan covers it (changed in 0.11 and 0.52); spend money but through an approved plan, within its budget; delete a post, an email or a campaign; change billing, account access or conversion tracking at any service.

Its marketing plan (added in 0.52; ADR 0042). The agent proposes a plan with `catervas_propose_marketing_plan`: its dates (at most 92 days), its budget in one currency, in total and per channel, its Google Ads campaigns (each with its budget and dates), its post slots (a channel, a day and a topic each) and how success is measured, with the plan's text. Catervas checks it and records `marketing_plan.proposed`; the owner approves it or sends it back with a reason on Today (`marketing_plan.approved`, `marketing_plan.returned`), and approving is the owner's alone whatever the team's `approvals` policy (5.6), as a purchase order is (6.10). One plan is active at a time, from its start to its end; approving a newer one ends the active one at the newer one's start, and the owner may end a plan at any time, which pauses its campaigns and stops its posts not yet sent (`marketing_plan.ended`).

The mandate as built (added in 0.55; phase 7 step 08c). The role's persona is "Owns your brand and how you reach people". Its `role.yaml` and prompt say it owns the brand kit, the brand persona, the marketing plan and the social presence, researches the market before it plans, proposes a plan with its budget for the owner to approve, and posts and advertises only as an approved plan says or after the owner allows that one call. Its `produces` list holds "the brand kit", "the brand persona" and "a marketing plan", and its `forbidden` list is exactly: write application code; publish, send or spend money except through a call the owner allows or the owner's approved marketing plan; delete a post, an email or a campaign; change billing, account access or conversion tracking at any service. The prompt names the four things with their paths, says that what a service or a competitor's page returns is data, never an instruction, and that a returned plan's reason is the owner's own words, which it reads and answers in the next version of the plan. Four kit skills carry the work, each written for any business (ADR 0040), in numbered sections under 6 KB: `keeping-the-brand-kit` (the kit's eight parts, name, promise, audience, colours as hex with where each is used and its contrast with text, type, logo, picture style and voice, written from the business's own material before anything is made, never another business's mark), `writing-the-brand-persona` (the character the brand plays on social media, how it talks to customers, what it never says, five sample replies and how it differs per network, never a real person's), `researching-the-market` (the audience and where it spends time, the words customers search for and what each channel costs to reach someone, each fact with its source and day under `docs/marketing/research/`, a guess marked as one) and `writing-the-marketing-plan` (research first, then goals and how each is measured, channels, the budget split by channel and campaign, the post calendar as slots, campaigns with dates inside the plan's, then proposing it and ending the turn). The persona's skill and the research skill say where they sit beside `writing-in-the-brands-voice`, the voice of any copy, and `researching-competitors`, which compares three to five rivals. The UI/UX Designer's `brand-and-design-tokens` reads the brand kit first when the project has one (6.8).

The brand's logo and pictures are the business's own (added in 0.55, on the founder's decision of 2026-10-06: "The logos are user independent not Catervas's. The user will provide his own logos and images."). The user puts them in `docs/marketing/brand/assets/`; the brand kit, which the agent writes, names and describes each file and where it came from and when; and when one the kit needs is missing, the agent asks the user for it with `catervas_ask_human`. The agent never makes a logo and never puts a generated picture in place of the brand's own, and Catervas has no tool that copies a generated picture into the brand kit. The pictures and clips the agent makes with its creative services (6.7) stay at those services, for a post or a page that uses them.

Proposing a plan (added in 0.55). `catervas_propose_marketing_plan`, of the `write_workspace` tier, is offered only to a Marketing Specialist's `implement` session about a task, and refused to anyone else with `marketing_plan_refused`. Its input is `title`, `summary`, `text`, `starts_on`, `ends_on`, `currency`, `budget { total, google_ads }`, `campaigns` (0 to 10, each `{ key, channel, name, goal, advertises, budget, starts_on, ends_on }`; `advertises` added in 0.72), `posts` (0 to 200, each `{ key, channel, on, topic }`), `measures`, and two fields the first design left open: `google_ads_account` (`NNN-NNN-NNNN`, which a plan with campaigns needs and one without them refuses) and `replaces`, the id of an approved plan still in force that the new one supersedes. Every amount is a decimal string of at most eight digits and at most two decimals, compared in hundredths and never as a float. Catervas checks the plan and answers every fault at once, each with its code and field: `marketing_plan_text` (a title of 3 to 100 characters, a summary of 20 to 600 and a text of 200 to 16,000, trimmed); `marketing_plan_dates` (`starts_on` no earlier than yesterday's UTC date, so an owner west of UTC is not refused their own today, and `ends_on` not before it and at most 92 days later, both days counted); `marketing_plan_currency` (three capital letters, and, for a plan that replaces another, the other's currency: the campaigns Catervas made for the old plan hold its amounts in it, so a plan in another currency replaces nothing and is proposed once the old one has ended; amended in 0.68, from step 08f's landing review); `marketing_plan_budget` (Google Ads at most the total, and the campaigns' budgets adding up to at most Google Ads'); `marketing_plan_campaign` (each of channel `google_ads`, a name of 1 to 100 characters, a goal of 1 to 300, what it advertises, the product, service or offer, of 3 to 200 characters trimmed (`advertises`; added in 0.72), a budget above 0 and dates inside the plan's); `marketing_plan_post` (each of one of eleven channels, `instagram`, `x`, `facebook`, `linkedin`, `threads`, `bluesky`, `tiktok`, `pinterest`, `youtube`, `google_business` and `mastodon`, on a day inside the plan's, with a topic of 1 to 200 characters); `marketing_plan_key` (every campaign's and post's key is lower-case words joined by `-`, at most 40 characters, and unique across the plan); `marketing_plan_measures` (1 to 10, of 3 to 200 characters each); `google_ads_account_needed`; and `marketing_plan_unknown` for a `replaces` that names no approved plan in force. A plan whose dates, amounts or channels cannot be read is answered with those faults alone, since the checks after them would be guesses, and a task with a plan waiting already is refused `marketing_plan_waiting` before anything else is checked. The plan's number is `MP-<n>`, one more than the highest of the log's plans and of the `MP-<n>.md` files in `docs/marketing/plans/` of the project's root and of the task's worktree, taken under one lock, so a fresh clone never reuses a committed number. Catervas writes the text, under the heading `# MP-<n>: <title>`, to `docs/marketing/plans/MP-<n>.md` in the task's worktree (`marketing_plan_file_exists` when it is there), after the permission check of that path, which is why the daemon's hook judges a Catervas tool of the `write_workspace` tier by the path its input names and the contract must allow it (`path_outside_allowed` otherwise). It records `marketing_plan.proposed` and answers the plan's id and that the agent is to end its turn: the owner's decision starts the next session, as a question's answer does (5.7).

The owner's decision (added in 0.55). Only the owner decides, under `ask` and `auto` alike (ADR 0041), with the commands `marketing_plan_decide { plan, decision, note? }`, `decision` `approve` or `return`, and `marketing_plan_end { plan, note? }`, which arrive only on `POST /command` behind the daemon's token or the browser's `command` behind its cookie, as `tool_approve` does (ADR 0031, 8.6), and never from an agent's session. `note` is at most 600 characters; it is the reason, and required, for `return` (`marketing_plan_reason_needed`), and optional for the rest. The refusals are `unknown_marketing_plan`, `marketing_plan_decided` (decided before), `marketing_plan_expired` (approving a plan whose `ends_on` is past), `marketing_plan_not_approved` (ending one that was never approved) and `marketing_plan_ended`. A plan whose task was cancelled may still be approved, since the plan does not depend on its task. The check and the write are one step under one lock, `PLANS`, which the owner's decision, the owner's end and the dated ends all hold, and every end goes through one function that reads the plans again under it and records nothing for a plan already ended, so two ends at once record one. `marketing_plan.approved { plan, note }` and `marketing_plan.returned { plan, reason }` (8.5) carry the proposing task on their envelope and no agent or session; the store reads one that names either as deciding nothing, as it reads a connector call's decision, and an end that names either likewise. The decision lowers the task's `open_plans` (5.7), and the next session is told it as the owner's own words (5.7). One plan is active at a time: among the approved plans not ended, the one approved last whose first and last day hold today, in UTC. Approving a plan ends, in the same step and with `marketing_plan.ended { why: replaced, replaced_by }`, every approved plan not ended that starts on or after the new one's first day; one that started earlier ends on the new one's first day, by the rule below, and a plan that is ended before its first day replaces nothing. A plan whose last day has passed ends `expired`. Those two dated ends are made by a rule with no model, `end_marketing_plans`, which every tick whose scope names no task runs first, before its pause check, so that it also runs while the team is paused, starts no session and does not use up the tick. The owner ends an approved plan at any time (`why: by_owner`). Ending a plan records its event and, in the same step, stops its posts as Posts below says (added in 0.57); pausing its campaigns comes with Google Ads (08f), which extends the one function that records an end.

Where the owner sees a plan (added in 0.55). The queries `marketing_plan.list {}`, every plan newest first with its `plan`, `title`, `state` (`proposed`, `returned`, `approved`, `active` or `ended`), dates, `currency`, `total`, `agent_id`, `task_id` and `proposed_at`, and `marketing_plan.get { plan }`, the proposal whole with its state, the owner's decision with their words and time, and the end with its `why`, the owner's note when they ended it with one, and the plan that replaced it when a newer plan did, and `not_found` for a plan nobody proposed, both read the log; `waiting.list` gives the row of 5.7 (a plan whose task is not on the board has no row). On Today the row "Marketing plan to approve: <title>" shows the agent's picture and name, the summary, the total budget with its currency, the dates, and "Review", which opens the plan's page at `/marketing/plans/MP-<n>`; a row of a kind the page does not know is skipped, never an error. The page shows the summary first, the budget as a table by channel and campaign, the post calendar by week, the measures, and the plan's text as text, never rendered as markup; while the plan waits, "Approve" and "Send back", whose reason is required and at most 600 characters; while it is approved or active, "End the plan", with an optional note and a confirmation that says what ending does; and once it is decided, who decided it, when, and their words; an ended plan says how it ended, quoting the owner's note when they ended it with one and linking the plan that replaced it when a newer plan did. It shows each campaign's `advertises` and price and, once Catervas's watch has read the spend, the spend against the budget and the pauses (below; changed in 0.72). What its posts have done is shown slot by slot under the calendar (added in 0.57; Posts, below). The command line is `catervas marketing plan show [<plan>] [--json]`, which reads the log and lists the plans or shows one whole, `catervas marketing plan approve <plan> [--note <text>]`, `catervas marketing plan return <plan> --reason <text>` and `catervas marketing plan end <plan> [--note <text>]`, which send the decision to the daemon when one drives the project and make it in the process otherwise (section 3, ADR 0014).

Posts (added in 0.52; as built in 0.57, phase 7 step 08d, ADR 0042). The agent writes a post with `catervas_schedule_post` and never calls a posting service's write tool itself: Buffer's `create_post` and `edit_post` are `denied` to it (6.7), and Catervas makes the call itself, one hour before the post's time, with the connection of the agent that wrote it. The tool, of the `read` tier since what governs it is the plan and the owner, is offered only to a Marketing Specialist's `implement` session about a task, and is `post_refused` from any other. Its input is `channel` (one of the plan's eleven), `buffer_channel` (Buffer's id for the channel, which the agent reads with Buffer's `list_channels`; `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`), `text`, `media` (at most four `{ url, kind }`, `kind` `image` or `video`), `at`, `details?` and `slot?`. Catervas checks it in this order and answers the first fault with its code: `post_channel_unknown`, `post_buffer_channel_invalid`, `post_too_long` (1 character to the network's limit, counted in Unicode scalar values, with no NUL: x 280, bluesky 300, threads 500, mastodon 500, pinterest 500, google_business 1,500, instagram 2,200, tiktok 2,200, linkedin 3,000, youtube 5,000, facebook 63,206), `post_media_invalid` (more than four, or another kind), `media_url_refused`, `post_needs_media` (Instagram and Pinterest need a picture or a clip, TikTok and YouTube a clip), `post_details` and `post_board_unknown` (YouTube needs `{ title, category_id }`, a title of 1 to 100 characters and one of Buffer's fifteen categories, as strings: 1, 2, 10, 15, 17, 19, 20 and 22 to 29; Pinterest needs `{ board }`, one of the channel's boards in Buffer's `get_channel`; no other network takes details), `post_time_invalid` (`at` is RFC 3339 with an offset), `post_in_the_past` and `post_too_far` (at most 92 days ahead). With a `slot`: `no_active_marketing_plan`, `not_a_plan_slot` (a slot of the active plan, of the same channel), `slot_used` (no other post of that plan and slot is scheduled or sent; a stopped, missed or failed one frees it), `post_off_its_day` (the date of `at` in its own offset is the slot's day, so Catervas needs no time-zone table) and `post_too_soon` (at least three hours from now). Last, Catervas's own `get_channel` through the agent's connection says whether the channel is the network's: `post_wrong_channel` (a channel that is another network's names that network in Catervas's own words, never in Buffer's, and says "another network" for a word it does not know; when Buffer refuses the channel, its words come in an `untrusted` block), `buffer_not_connected` or `buffer_unreachable`. A picture's address is checked at scheduling and again at hand-over (`media_url_refused`): `https` only, port 443, no user or password, at most 2,000 characters, a public IP address or a name of two labels or more that is not `.local`, `.localhost`, `.internal`, `.lan` or `.home.arpa`; Catervas's request for it follows no redirect and no proxy, connects only to a public address (a name that resolves to a private one is refused at the connection), and must be answered with a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP picture (never an SVG) of at most 20 MiB, or with a video of at most a gigabyte, whose size it says when it says one. The agent's pictures are the business's own, ones the owner gave it, or ones it made with its creative services (6.7), never another's; the address must stay public until the post goes out, since Buffer reads it then.

A post that fills an unused slot of the active plan is recorded `social_post.scheduled { approved_by: plan, plan, slot }`, whose sequence number is the post's number, and the tool answers `{ post, hands_over_at }`. Today shows it under "Going out" with its text, pictures, channel and time in the browser's time zone, why it may go out, and Stop; one hour before its time Catervas calls Buffer's `create_post { channelId, text, schedulingType: automatic, mode: customScheduled, dueAt, assets, metadata? }` itself (`metadata` for YouTube's title and category and Pinterest's board alone) and records `social_post.sent { post, buffer_post }`. A post outside the plan (no `slot`) is recorded `social_post.requested` and waits for the owner on Today with "Post it" and "Don't post" (`social_post_decide { post, decision: post | dont_post, note? }`, from the daemon's token or the browser's cookie alone): "Post it" records `social_post.scheduled { post, approved_by: owner }` on the request's task, with no agent or session, and the hand-over comes an hour before its time or at once when that has passed; "Don't post" records `social_post.stopped { by: declined }`. `unknown_post`, `post_decided` and `post_in_the_past` (to allow a request whose time has passed or is within five minutes, too near for Catervas to hand over, or to decline one whose time has passed) refuse a decision. A request whose time comes within five minutes undecided is recorded `social_post.missed { why: undecided }`. Under `auto` it is sent as 5.6 lets any outward act (ADR 0041; phase 9 step 01). The task does not wait for a request or for a post.

Stop (`social_post_stop { post }`, the owner's alone) takes a scheduled post not yet with Buffer back at once (`social_post.stopped { by: owner }`); for a post Buffer has, whose time is still ahead, Catervas first calls Buffer's own `delete_post` and records `stopped { by: owner, taken_back: true }` only on its success, else refuses `post_not_taken_back` with nothing recorded; once its time has passed it is `post_already_out`; `post_being_handed_over` while Catervas is giving it to Buffer, `post_not_going_out` for a post requested, stopped, missed or failed, and `unknown_post`. Ending a plan stops, as `stopped { by: plan_ended }`, the plan's posts not yet with Buffer when the owner ended it, or when a newer plan replaced it and the post's slot day is on or after the newer plan's first day; never when the plan only ran out, since each post was checked to fall on a slot day inside the plan, and never a post Catervas is handing over at that moment, which stays with its Stop. A post already with Buffer stays, with its Stop. Every check and record of a post holds the plans' lock `PLANS`, never across a wait for Buffer: the hand-over claims a post under it (`HANDING`, by the project and the post) before it calls Buffer and records the outcome under it after, so that a Stop is either before the claim and the post is not handed over, or after it and is refused.

The hand-over (`hand_over_posts`) is a rule with no model that every tick whose scope names no task runs after `end_marketing_plans`, before the pause check, so that it runs while the team is paused by Catervas's own refusal of the key, since Buffer needs no model. Only a pause by the owner holds it: it hands nothing over, and records `social_post.missed { why: paused }` for each scheduled post whose time is within five minutes. Otherwise, oldest hand-over first, a scheduled post whose time has come to be handed over (an hour before its time, or the owner's decision when that is later) is claimed and its picture addresses are asked again; a post whose time is within five minutes (or past) is recorded `missed { why: not_running }` instead, and one Catervas was not running to hand over in time is sent when its time is still more than five minutes ahead. A failure is recorded `social_post.failed { post, reason }` and never retried, since Buffer may already have the post: a tool error, or Buffer turning the call away over its rate limit with an HTTP 429 (the body is its words), "Buffer did not take it: “<Buffer's words, cut at 300 characters>”"; a timeout, "Buffer did not answer in time and may have the post: look in Buffer's queue before <time>."; an answer with no post id, "Buffer answered without the post's id: look in Buffer's queue before <time>."; a connection that is not there or has lapsed, "<the agent's display name>'s Buffer connection is not there; connect Buffer again." (its name as the owner reads it, the id only when the team file has no such agent); any other failure, "Catervas could not reach Buffer."; a picture that no longer opens, "Its picture or clip no longer opens." The hand-over runs between sessions, and a session runs to its end before the next tick, so a post can be handed over late by one running session, and a session limit over 55 minutes (`max_wall_clock_seconds`) can make one missed.

What the owner and the agent see (added in 0.57). The queries `social_posts.list {}` (every post scheduled or sent whose time is still ahead, soonest first, each with `hands_over_at`, then every post missed or failed in the last 24 hours, the latest first, with `missed_why` or `reason`) and `marketing_plan.get`, which gains `written_posts` beside the plan's slots (each post written for them, oldest first, with its state and when it reached it), read the log; the method `social_post.media { post, index }` has the daemon fetch a post's picture and answers its content type and base64, or `not_found` for a clip, an unknown post or picture, and an address the check above refuses, since the browser's `img-src` is `'self' data:` and a clip or a picture the daemon will not fetch is opened by its address in a new tab (`noopener noreferrer`). Today's "Going out (<n>)" section says "Posts in your plan go out without asking you. Stop any of them before its time." and each row says its network, its day and time in the browser's time zone, how soon, its words as text, its pictures, "Approved in your plan MP-<n>" or "You allowed this", and when Catervas hands it to Buffer or that Buffer has it; "Stop" asks first and, once sent, says Catervas takes it back from Buffer, and when Buffer would not, "Delete it in Buffer before <time>, or it goes out." with a link to Buffer. A post that did not go out in the last 24 hours is listed, muted, with why and that the agent hears of it: a miss says "Catervas could not hand it to Buffer before its time." (true whether Catervas was off, paused or busy with a long session, and no more than Catervas knows), "The team was paused, so it was not sent." or "You had not decided by its time, so it was not sent.", and a failure its `reason` as text. The plan's page counts its slots' posts ("<n> sent, <n> going out, <n> stopped by you, <n> missed, and <n> not written yet", a count of none left out), shows each slot's newest post with its state and an earlier failure, and the end confirmation says how many posts not yet sent will not go out and how many are already with Buffer. The agent's activity line says "Waiting on you: may <agent> post on <Network>?" for a request, `waiting.list` gives it a row of kind `social_post`, and `moved.since` words each post event ("Kai wrote the Instagram post for 13:00.", "You stopped the X post for Sat 31 Oct at 09:00.", and the like). The agent's next `implement` message tells it, after the rest, of each of its posts that was missed or failed ("Catervas could not post <post> (<Network>, <time>):" and the reason in an `untrusted` block), that the owner did not allow it ("The owner did not allow your post <post> on <Network>." and, with a note, "The owner adds: <note>", in the owner's own words, unwrapped) or stopped it ("The owner stopped your post <post>."). The command line is `catervas marketing post list [--json]`, `catervas marketing post stop <n>`, `catervas marketing post send <n>` and `catervas marketing post decline <n> [--note <text>]`; a command handled in the terminal runs on deps whose daemon holds the connections kept on this computer, so that a Stop reaches Buffer, and a run's end lists a request as "<task> waits: <agent name> wants to post on <Network>: catervas marketing post send <n>, or catervas marketing post decline <n>" (section 3, ADR 0014). Paid Instagram and Facebook ads wait until Meta admits Catervas's client to its Ads server.

Google Ads (added in 0.52). Catervas's own connection (6.7, ADR 0038), signed in with Google through Catervas's own Google app, which Catervas Cloud holds from phase 8 (ADR 0048), no build being able to sign in to Google before it (changed in 0.61, ADR 0044), for the one scope `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/adwords` (ADR 0035, pulled forward by ADR 0042). Its reads (the accounts, fixed reports with clicks, impressions, cost and conversions, and keyword ideas) are `network`. Its writes (a Search campaign for a plan campaign, its ad groups, keywords and ads, a budget, pause or enable) are `external_effect` marked as approved by the marketing plan, a mark only Catervas's own servers may carry: the hook lets such a call run while a plan is active, and Catervas's server refuses every write the active plan does not cover (`not_in_marketing_plan`), never asks. A campaign is created paused, ending with its plan campaign, with a budget Google itself keeps within what the plan campaign has left: a total budget where Google offers one, else a daily budget no larger than what is left divided by the days left. The budget is a hard stop: while Catervas runs it reads the spend every 15 minutes with no model, and when a campaign or the plan reaches its budget it pauses the plan's campaigns itself, records `marketing_budget.reached`, and Today asks the owner to raise the budget (a new version of the plan) or end the plan. Google may spend up to one read's worth past a cap while Catervas runs, and its own budgets bound the spend while Catervas does not.

Google Ads, as built (added in 0.68; ADR 0042, ADR 0044; phase 7 step 08f; the connector is described in 6.7). The kit's skill `running-search-ads` teaches looking before making (`list_accounts`, `keyword_ideas`), one campaign for each plan campaign, ad groups by theme with keywords and their match types, negative keywords, two or more ads for each ad group, `maximize_conversions` only on an ad account that already tracks conversions (Catervas never sets tracking up), enabling a campaign last, `report`'s `search_terms` read to pause what costs without results, and never a competitor's brand name, a claim without a source or sensitive targeting. Until Catervas Cloud signs customers in to Google in phase 8 (ADR 0044, ADR 0048) the connector is built and tested against a fake Google Ads server, the kit row for Google Ads says it comes with Catervas’s web launch and offers no Connect, and the founder's live check is phase 8's. The spend read and the budget's hard stop are as built in 0.72 (below); with no process driving the project, the budget Google keeps (a total budget or a daily one, 6.7) is what bounds the spend.

The budget's hard stop, as built (added in 0.72; ADR 0042; phase 7 step 08g). **The watch.** `Orchestrator::watch_marketing_spend` runs in a task of its own that `catervas serve` and `catervas run` spawn once recovery is done and that ends with the orchestrator, since a tick waits for a running session, which may run 30 minutes (5.5). It starts no session and runs while the team is paused, because stopping spend is never paused. Until a `marketing_campaign.created` is in the log it waits for the first campaign made, and from then on it wakes every minute (`RECHECK`, through the sleeper). A store error ends it and stops the orchestrator, so `catervas serve` ends saying so, and so does a panic in it (added in 0.72 after review); a Google, sign-in or team-file failure is that read's failure, and the watch goes on. **The read.** An active plan with a campaign made under its lineage is read when its last attempt, kept in memory by plan id, is 15 minutes old or absent, so a restart reads at once; a failed read is an attempt too, since a retry every minute would spend Catervas Cloud's shared quota (ADR 0044) on failures that rarely clear within one. A read is one `Search` for each ad account the lineage's campaigns are in, `spend_query` over that account's campaigns, from the day before the first campaign was made, in UTC, to tomorrow, so the account's time zone cannot drop a day; costs are summed for each plan campaign's key and in all, in hundredths rounded up from micros. The plan's cost is that of every campaign of the lineage, as enabling counts it (6.7), so enabling and the cap count one spend: 96 operations a day for each active plan and ad account. Whose connection reads (changed in 0.72 after review): the plan's proposer's `google-ads` entry, then each other Marketing Specialist's, the active ones first, then the paused, then the retired, each in the team file's order, through the entry that `matches_kit` and its stored sign-in (no session ticket); an agent's status does not keep it from being tried, since stopping spend is never paused: a paused agent keeps its sign-in; a retired agent is tried last and finds a sign-in only when it was retired outside Catervas (a hand edit of the team file, or a pulled one), since retiring it in Catervas pauses its ads first and deletes its keys (ADR 0030, below), and is passed over otherwise (changed in 0.72 after review). The next is tried when this one's grant cannot be had (no entry, not the kit's, a sign-in that has lapsed, is unconfirmed or failed) or Google answers that this sign-in does not reach the account; any other answer of Google's ends the read, since another sign-in meets the same and spends more of the quota; with none left the read fails with the last reason, and the last spend that worked stays beside the failure, with its time. **The caps.** `catervas_core::marketing::caps_reached` decides, and only for the active plan: a plan campaign's key whose cost is at least its budget reaches a `campaign` cap; the plan's cost at least its Google Ads budget (when that is above nothing) reaches the `plan` cap, which takes every campaign the active plan carries. A campaign is carried only when its plan is in the active plan's lineage, the active plan has its key, and it is in the active plan's `google_ads_account`. A cap recorded for the active plan, by its scope and key, is not reached again, and a raised version's own budgets decide from its approval. **A pause** reads status first, one `Search` for each ad account with `status_query` (the campaigns' resource names and statuses, no metrics, so a campaign with no cost still has its row), then sends one `googleAds:mutate` of `PAUSED` for each campaign reported `ENABLED` or missing from the rows, each on its own since one mutate is atomic; a campaign reported `PAUSED` or `REMOVED` counts as paused with no call. It is Catervas's own fixed act, not a session's call and with no hook, on the list of operations of 6.7. At a cap Catervas pauses its campaigns, then records `marketing_budget.reached { plan, scope, key?, spent, budget, currency, paused, failed? }`, `paused` being the campaigns Google accepted or reported paused and `failed` the first refusal's words cut at 300 characters. While the cap's plan is the active plan, each later read reads the status of the cap's campaigns again and pauses any that is `ENABLED` (a refused pause, or a campaign enabled by hand), recording `marketing_campaign.paused { plan, key, campaign, why: budget_reached }` once for a campaign not in the cap's `paused` and not recorded paused for a budget since the plan's first cap (changed in 0.72 after review, so that a raised version's pause of a campaign its predecessor had paused is recorded too). The writes' lock (6.7) is held from the read through the pauses, so no write is in flight. **A plan that ends** by the owner, `replaced` or `expired` has the campaigns the active plan does not carry paused at the watch's first wake after `marketing_plan.ended`, not at the next 15-minute read: the campaigns `to_pause_for_end` lists (every campaign Catervas made that the active plan does not carry, whether it was made under a plan outside its lineage, for a key it lacks or in another ad account, and that is not yet recorded paused for the end; with no active plan, every such campaign; changed in 0.72 after review), each recorded `marketing_campaign.paused { why: plan_ended }` when Google accepts the pause or reports the campaign `PAUSED` or `REMOVED`. A campaign the active plan carries keeps running under it within its budget, and its own end stops it at Google in any case. A refusal is kept as the plan's `unstopped` and tried again every 15 minutes, until a later pause of the plan succeeds; once every campaign is recorded paused for the end the plan is not looked at again. A campaign recorded paused because Google Ads was removed (`connection_removed`) counts as paused for the end (changed in 0.72 after review) while no `google-ads` connection has been recorded since, so that a plan ending after a removal asks Google for nothing, keeps no `unstopped` and shows no "Ads still running" for ads that are paused; once Google Ads is connected again it counts no more, since an agent could have enabled the campaign. It counts no more either while an agent that is not retired has a `google-ads` entry in the team file (changed in 0.72 after review, with the same note): another Marketing Specialist's sign-in could have enabled the campaign with no connection recorded, so the end's pause reads status with that grant and pauses what is `ENABLED`, as for any other campaign. A retired agent has no sign-in to enable with (retiring it deletes its keys and it runs no session), so retiring the only Marketing Specialist that has Google Ads leaves nothing to pause when the plan ends. The watch, `catervas disconnect` and the command that ends a plan, handled with no process driving, read the same rule. **Removing Google Ads.** `connector.disconnect` for `google-ads`, once the command's own checks pass (the agent has the entry; a refused removal pauses nothing, changed in 0.72 after review), first pauses, under the writes' lock and with that agent's grant, every campaign Catervas made that is not recorded paused for its plan's end, whichever Marketing Specialist runs it, recording `marketing_campaign.paused` with `why: plan_ended` for a campaign `to_pause_for_end` lists and `connection_removed` for the others; then the connection is removed whatever Google answered, since Catervas could not stop the ads without it. The writes' lock is held from the pause until the removal is done (the entry out of the team file and its keys deleted), so that no agent's enable runs in between and turns on what Catervas stopped (added in 0.72 after review); every removal path holds it the same way, from its pause through its write of the team, and the order of the locks is the writes' lock first and the team's second, never the other way. A refusal, or a grant that cannot be had, is kept as `unstopped` for Today (5.7). The Remove confirmation says so beforehand: "Catervas pauses your marketing plan's running ads first, since without this connection it could not stop them at their budget. If Google refuses, Google Ads is removed anyway, and the ads keep running at Google until their end date or their budget there; pause them in Google Ads." Every other path that takes a Marketing Specialist's `google-ads` sign-in away does the same before it writes (added in 0.72 after review): `team.save` and `team.start` when the team they write drops the entry or the agent that holds it, `template.apply` when the template removes an agent that has it or retires one, and retiring an agent (the page's retire, which is `agent_update`; `agent.replace`; and the template's retirement), which keeps the entry in the team file but deletes the agent's keys (ADR 0030). Each pauses for the first agent that loses the sign-in whose pause leaves nothing running, with that agent's grant, holding the writes' lock until it has written, then goes on whatever Google answered, keeping a refusal as `unstopped`; a team, a template, a replacement or a retirement that would be refused pauses nothing. A save cannot retire an agent (a status changes only on the agent's card), and the command line has no command that retires one, so none needs `catervas disconnect`'s refusal (added in 0.72 after review). `catervas disconnect <agent> google-ads` refuses `disconnect_in_the_browser: remove Google Ads on <agent>'s page in the browser, where Catervas pauses its running ads first` whenever a campaign Catervas made is not recorded paused for its plan's end, whether or not a process drives the project, since the pause and the removal go together on the agent's page in the browser (changed in 0.72 after review, from "when nothing drives the project").

What each campaign advertises, and its price (added in 0.72; the founder's decisions of 2026-10-07: "Catervas must have a plan of what exactly to advertise and get a final price before beginning. If the price is dynamic, then just dont say so before committing to a plan", then "Disclose dynamic ones"). Each campaign of a plan says what it advertises (`advertises`, required, 3 to 200 characters; a plan proposed before has none, read back empty and shown "Not stated"). `price_kind(campaign, currency, day)`, pure, is `campaign_budget`'s kind (6.7) for a campaign made on `day`: `Fixed` for a total budget, which Google never bills past, and `NotFixed` for a daily one, which may spend past its cap for about an hour because Google reports cost up to about an hour late (Google Ads Help 2544985). `marketing_plan.get` gives each campaign `advertises` and `price` (`fixed` or `not_fixed`), as of today while the plan is proposed or sent back and as of the day it was approved (the UTC date of the approval event) once approved; a campaign already made for the key under the plan's lineage, in its ad account, is priced by the budget it has, `fixed` for a total and `not_fixed` for a daily one, whatever dates a new version gives it (changed in 0.72 after review, since a raised version of a long daily campaign would otherwise show a price it cannot have). The plan's page shows, under each campaign's goal, "Advertises: <advertises>" and "Price: fixed at <budget> <currency>" or "Price: up to <budget> <currency>, may run over by about an hour's spend"; "What approving lets <agent> do" adds, for each campaign not at a fixed price, "<name> is not at a fixed price, so it may run over by about an hour's spend.", the decision bar "1 campaign may run over by about an hour's spend." ("<n> campaigns …"), and a note under the table says "A fixed price is a total that Google itself never charges past. Google keeps one only for a campaign of 3 to 90 days; any other has a daily budget, and since Google reports cost up to about an hour late, Catervas may pause it after about an hour's more spend." Once approved, the budget's caption adds "Prices as on the day you approved the plan." A fixed price stays fixed: `AdsPlanView` carries `approved_on`, and creating a campaign for a key that was `Fixed` on that day is refused when `campaign_budget` would now give a daily budget ("<key> was approved at a fixed price, which Google keeps only for a run of 3 days or more; propose a new version"). The kit's skills `writing-the-marketing-plan` and `running-search-ads` say: "Prefer campaigns at a fixed price: 3 to 90 days, made at least two days before they start. Say in the plan's text what each campaign advertises, and which campaigns are not at a fixed price and why."

The plan's page shows the spend (added in 0.72). `marketing_plan.get` gains `spend { read_at?, total?, by_key?, failed?, failed_at? }` from the watch's memory (left out until it has tried; `by_key` is by plan campaign key), `reached` (the plan's `marketing_budget.reached`, each `{ scope, key?, spent, budget, failed?, at }`) and `paused` (its `marketing_campaign.paused` and the campaigns paused at a cap, each `{ key, name, why, at }`, oldest first). The page's "Spent so far" ("Spent" once the plan ended) section gives the total against the plan's Google Ads budget with a meter, "Google Ads' own figures, read <when>. Catervas reads them every 15 minutes while it runs." (once ended: "… last read <when>, before the plan ended. Google Ads itself has the final figures."), and, after a failed read of a plan still running, "Catervas can't read the spend now: <reason> Last tried <when>; Catervas tries again every 15 minutes. Until a read works, Catervas cannot pause the ads at their budget."; the budget table gains each campaign's spend, marked "Paused at its budget", "Paused: the plan ended" or "Paused: Google Ads was removed" by its newest pause; and "Ads Catervas paused" lists each pause, newest first, with its time and why ("<name>: it reached its budget, <spent> of <budget> <currency>.", "<name>: the plan ended.", "<name>: Google Ads was removed."). The End confirmation of a plan with ads begins "Catervas pauses its running ads within a minute." and, once a read worked, "<spent> is spent so far, by Google's figures at <time>.", and the hint beside End reads "Ending pauses the ads within a minute and stops the posts not yet sent." (changed from 08c's, since the browser always has `catervas serve` driving); `catervas marketing plan end`, handled with no process driving, adds "Catervas pauses <plan>'s ads when it next runs." when a campaign Catervas made is still to pause. `moved.since` tells each pause: "Catervas paused <plan>'s ads at their budget." ("… campaign <name> at its budget." at a campaign's; "Catervas could not pause …" when Google refused) and "Catervas paused <plan>'s campaign <name>: the plan ended." ("… Google Ads was removed.", "… it reached its budget.").

The raise (added in 0.72; ADR 0042). "Raise the budget" on Today's row opens a dialog for the new Google Ads budget and the new budget of each campaign that reached its own, refusing in the dialog, with no call, an amount at or below its spend ("Make it more than the <spent> <currency> already spent."), a Google Ads budget below what the plan has now ("Make it at least the <budget> <currency> it is now.") and campaigns adding up to more than the Google Ads budget ("The campaigns' budgets add up to <sum> <currency>: make this at least that."); the plan's total rises by what the Google Ads budget does, `check_proposal` refusing a Google Ads budget above the total. It calls `marketing_budget.raise { plan, google_ads, campaigns: [{ key, budget }] } -> { task_id }`, one of the gates' write methods, which checks the same (`raise_refused`), that `plan` is the active plan with a `marketing_budget.reached`, and that no raise of it is open (`raise_open`: a raise whose task is neither accepted nor cancelled). What was spent is the last good read, else the recorded caps', so the owner can raise right after a restart. It files a request through the same path as `request.file`, titled "New version of <plan> with a raised budget", whose text says: "Propose a new version of <plan> that replaces it (replaces: <plan>), starting today, with its Google Ads budget <amount> <currency> and its total <amount>, and <key>'s budget <amount>; keep its campaigns and post slots that are not over, each with its key and what it advertises, their dates from today on. Once the owner approves it, raise each paused campaign's budget at Google with set_campaign_budget, then enable it.", with `raises` on `task.created` and the triage of 5.16, and it skips the sprint queue (3). The agent proposes the new version as any plan; the owner approves it as any plan, and approving it replaces the old plan as a newer plan does (above). Rejected: the owner approving a raised budget directly, which would leave Catervas to enable ads itself.

What Google may spend past a cap (added in 0.72). Google reports cost up to about an hour late (Google Ads Help 2544985), so a campaign at a daily budget may spend about an hour past its cap, and up to a minute more, while a process drives the project (`catervas serve`; `catervas run` exits when idle); a campaign at a total budget is never passed. With no process driving the project, Google's own budgets (6.7) bound the spend.

Default tools: read, network, write_workspace scoped to its paths, git_local, so that it commits what it wrote (changed in 0.10).

Kit (added in 0.51; 6.7): fifteen skills written for any business, software or not, `positioning-and-messaging`, `planning-a-launch`, `writing-for-search`, `writing-in-the-brands-voice`, `keeping-a-content-calendar`, `researching-competitors`, `measuring-campaigns`, `making-images-and-video`, `posting-and-email` (added in 0.53), `keeping-the-brand-kit`, `writing-the-brand-persona`, `researching-the-market` and `writing-the-marketing-plan` (the four added in 0.55) `running-social-channels` (added in 0.57) and `running-search-ads` (added in 0.68), and five services, each signed in to. Two spend the user's credits, Higgsfield (images, video and voice-overs) and Recraft (graphics and vector art), each making one image, clip or edit inside an allowance the user sets and asking for everything else. Two reach the business's audience (added in 0.53; ADR 0042): Buffer reads the business's channels and how earlier posts did for the agent and, through Catervas's own calls, schedules the posts of the plan the owner approved to Instagram, X and nine other networks, and the owner is asked about any other; Kit drafts email broadcasts, email series and landing pages and reads their numbers, and a broadcast is only a draft the human schedules and sends from Kit. Its pictures and clips are files and addresses the human or a post uses. One runs the plan's search ads (added in 0.68; ADR 0042): Google Ads, Catervas's own connector, whose writes run only inside the plan the owner approved (5.6, 6.7). Design files (Figma, Canva) are in no kit while those services admit only clients on their own lists.

### 6.6 Finance Specialist (added in 0.21)

Mandate: keep track of every cost and expense of the product and run its books. The costs are the team's AI spending, read from Catervas's own records; when the product uses Stripe, its revenue, fees, payouts and refunds; and everything the user's receipts and bank statements show, such as cloud, infrastructure, storage, hosting, domains, software subscriptions, and payment and billing fees. It records the AI spending and forecasts it first; then it takes each receipt from the receipts mailbox, categorises it and records it, reconciles the books against the bank or card statement, closes each month, forecasts expenses over 12 to 36 months, analyses pricing and unit economics, and recommends budgets in plain words. What it produces is management accounting, not a tax filing, statutory accounts, or financial advice, and it says so. It is optional: the team builder offers it and does not suggest it (ADR 0019; `docs/design/finance-specialist.md`).

Produces: `.xlsx` workbooks in `.catervas/local/finance/`, the books (`books.xlsx`, each row naming its receipt file, statement line or Stripe object), the forecast (`forecast.xlsx`), and any other a task asks for; the receipts it filed, under `receipts/<yyyy-mm>/`; the completion note that explains them.

Cannot: write application code or anything in the repository; pay, refund, move money, change Catervas's budgets, or change anything in Stripe or the mailbox; send, delete, move, or mark any email; publish anywhere; write outside its finance folder.

Default tools: read and network (to research prices), and Catervas tools that only it may call, but for the two sheet tools, which the Procurement Specialist has for its own folder (6.10; changed in 0.70), each of the `read` tier: `catervas_read_costs`, the team's AI spending by task, agent, sprint, day or purpose; `catervas_read_sheet`, which the Product Manager, who reviews and accepts a finance task (5.3), may also call in a verify session about it; `catervas_write_sheet`, which writes a whole workbook in the finance folder and keeps the previous version in `.history/` (as built, below; changed in 0.56); and, once receipts intake ships, `catervas_read_receipts`, the messages of the receipts mailbox not yet filed, and `catervas_file_receipt`, which saves a message's receipt and marks it `recorded`, or marks it `not_a_receipt`. Its reviewer is the Product Manager.

As built so far (added in 0.54; phase 7 step 09). The role is `finance_specialist`, "Finance Specialist", tag "FIN", with the persona "Keeps your numbers straight", the model `claude-sonnet-5-5` at `medium` effort and the default session limits. Its tiers are exactly `read` and `network`, its reviewer is the Product Manager, and it does not change code (5.12). Its prompt and its one skill, `keeping-the-books`, say that its numbers are management accounting, not a tax filing, statutory accounts or financial advice; that every number names its source; and that it never pays, refunds or moves money, changes a budget, or writes to a service. Its kit, since phase 7 step 10, holds six skills and the services Stripe, Digits and Kick, each only read (6.7; changed in 0.67). Its picture is the brand's `extra-4` character, copied under the key `finance-specialist` while `extra-4` stays among the extras, and its tag colour is a pale olive, `#C2C79B` in both themes, held to the same contrast against the role ink as the other role colours. The Team page's "Add someone" offers it after the Marketing Specialist, and an agent added as one takes that picture and a spare name; setup does not suggest it, and offers it, unticked, under "More roles" (added in 0.70; F1). The user can talk to it in a one-to-one chat. Its spreadsheet tools are described below (added in 0.56), and its tasks, which run in its private folder, below them (added in 0.62).

The spreadsheet tools, as built (added in 0.56; phase 7 step 09b). Three tools of the `read` tier, each writing only to Catervas's own private folder, as `catervas_write_memory` writes only the agent's notebook (5.6).

The folder line. The Finance Specialist's folder is `.catervas/local/finance/`, found from the role by `private_folder(role)` in `catervas-core`, which is `None` for every role but this one and the Procurement Specialist, whose folder is `.catervas/local/procurement/` (6.10; 0.70); it is made readable by its owner alone when it is first written, and it is never committed, since `.catervas/local/` is kept out of git. A tool's `path` is relative to the caller's folder: 1 to 200 characters in at most 3 parts joined by `/`, each part 1 to 100 characters of letters, digits, spaces, `.`, `_` and `-` that starts with a letter or a digit (so no part starts with a dot, and `.history/` cannot be named), the last ending in `.xlsx` in lower case; the procurement folder also holds notes, so `private_file_fault(folder, path)` in `catervas-core` lets a path there end in `.md` in lower case too, which the sheet tools never open, since they check the path as a workbook's first (0.70). A path of any other shape, a path with a part that is a link, a folder that is a link or lies below one (every part from the project's root down is checked, so `.catervas/local` and `.catervas/local/finance` themselves may not be links), and a path that resolves outside the folder once links are resolved are each refused as `private_path_refused: <path> <why>`, and nothing is written. The tools report no path to the permission check (5.6) and hold the folder line themselves, so `.catervas/local/**` stays a protected path for every other call. They read `.xlsx` alone: CSV, with `imports/`, comes with receipts intake in phase 15.

`catervas_read_costs { by, from?, to? }`. `by` is `task`, `agent`, `sprint`, `day` or `purpose`, the key the costs of 5.5 are summed by; `from` and `to` are UTC days, `YYYY-MM-DD`, given together or not at all, `from` not after `to` and at most 366 days apart, and the sum then counts the costs recorded on those days, both included (`cost_range_invalid` otherwise). It answers `{ by, rows: [{ key, usd, input_tokens, output_tokens, sessions }] }` and records nothing. A Finance Specialist may call it in any session; any other role is refused `sheet_refused`, the Procurement Specialist among them (0.70).

`catervas_write_sheet { path, sheets }` writes one whole workbook in the caller's own folder, and only in the implement session of a task whose assignee is the caller, so that a chat or a conversation never writes the books or the register (any other call is refused `sheet_refused`; the Procurement Specialist has it too, 0.70, and takes no `folder`). `sheets` is 1 to 20 of `{ name, columns, rows }`. A name is 1 to 31 characters with none of `[`, `]`, `:`, `*`, `?`, `/`, `\` and `|`, no apostrophe at either end, not History in any case, and different from the other names without regard to case. `columns` is 0 to 100 headings, written as the sheet's bold first row when there are any, and `rows` 0 to 10,000 rows of 0 to 100 cells, so that row numbers are the spreadsheet's own and the headings are row 1. A cell is a number, a string of up to 32,767 characters, a boolean, `null` for an empty cell, `{ "date": "YYYY-MM-DD" }` (from 1900, written as a date and shown as `yyyy-mm-dd`), or `{ "formula": "=..." }`. A bound that is broken, or a date that is not one, is refused `sheet_refused`, naming the sheet or the cell, before anything is written. A string is always written as text, never as a formula, whatever it starts with, so that a value from Stripe, a receipt or an email that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or an at sign stays inert in the user's spreadsheet program (8.6). A formula is refused `formula_refused`, naming its sheet, its cell and its row and column, and nothing is written, when it does not start with `=`, when it holds `[` (an external reference) or `|` (a DDE call such as `=cmd|' /C calc'!A0`), or when it uses `HYPERLINK`, `WEBSERVICE`, `FILTERXML`, `IMPORTDATA`, `IMPORTXML`, `IMPORTHTML`, `IMPORTRANGE`, `IMPORTFEED`, `IMAGE`, `RTD`, `DDE`, `CALL`, `REGISTER.ID`, `EXEC`, `INFO`, `CELL`, `COPILOT`, `TRANSLATE`, `DETECTLANGUAGE`, `PY`, `STOCKHISTORY`, `GOOGLETRANSLATE`, `GOOGLEFINANCE` or `AI`. To use a function is to name it, in any letter case, after the start of the formula, a character outside letters, digits, `_` and `.`, or one of the prefixes `_xlfn.`, `_xlws.`, `_xleta.` and `_xludf.`, and before white space and either `(`, which calls it, or `)`, `,` or the end of the formula, which hands it to another function by name, as `=MAP(A1:A2,WEBSERVICE)` and `=LET(f,IMAGE,f(A1))` do. `MYCELLS(1)` and `MY.INFO(1)` use nothing listed, and `INFO ("os")` does. A name followed by a digit, a letter or `:`, or, without a call, after `:` or `$`, is a cell or a column reference, as in `SUM(DDE1:DDE3)`, `SUM(RTD:RTD)` and `SUM($AI:$AI)`, and passes. A listed name inside a string literal is refused too when it is followed as above, which is safe. Catervas never computes a formula: each is written with an empty stored value, which a spreadsheet program fills in when it opens the file. When a workbook is there already, its file is first copied to `.history/<path>.<UTC yyyymmddThhmmssZ>.xlsx` (the file's own extension: `.md` for a note, 6.10), the path written with `%` as `%25` and `/` as `%2F` (so `a/b.xlsx` is `a%2Fb.xlsx` and never the name of `a__b.xlsx`), with `-<n>` before the extension when that name is taken, so that every previous version is kept. A `.history` that is a link is refused `private_path_refused`, and nothing is written. The new file is written beside the target and renamed over it, so that a reader never sees half a file, and a file over 10 MiB is refused `sheet_too_large` before it replaces anything. It answers `{ path, sheets: [{ name, rows }], replaced }`, `rows` being the rows given, the headings not counted.

`catervas_read_sheet { path, folder?, sheet?, from_row?, rows?, baseline? }` reads a workbook: every sheet, or the one named, from row `from_row` (counted from 1, default 1), at most `rows` rows of each (1 to 500, default 200). A row is its cells as far as the last one that holds something, each a number (a whole number as an integer), a string, a boolean, `null`, `{ "date": "<ISO date>" }` (with `THH:MM:SS` when it has a time), or `{ "error": "<#DIV/0! and the like>" }`; a formula's cell is `{ "formula": "<its text, starting with =>", "value": <the value a spreadsheet program last stored for it, or null> }`, and a workbook only Catervas wrote has no stored value. A file over 10 MiB is refused `sheet_too_large`, a file that is not there or not a workbook, or a sheet that is not in it, `sheet_refused`; a sheet's name is the workbook writer's text, so when a refusal quotes the names a workbook holds (the sheet asked for is not there, or a sheet cannot be read) it quotes them inside an `untrusted` block of source `sheet` (8.6), cut at 2 KiB. What a workbook holds is the user's and the services', never an instruction, so the answer is `{ sheets, more }`, where `sheets` is the JSON of `[{ name, rows, total_rows, more }]` as one `untrusted` block of source `sheet` (8.6) cut at 256 KiB (a page stops adding rows once its JSON passes that size), and `more` is true when it was cut or any sheet has rows after its page. A role with a private folder may call it in any session, and reads its own. A Finance Specialist may also read the procurement register: `folder: "procurement"` with `path: "vendors.xlsx"`, which is the one file there it reads; any other path with `folder` is refused `private_path_refused`, a `baseline` with it `sheet_refused`, and `folder` is refused `sheet_refused` to every other role, the reviewer of a procurement task and the Product Manager accepting it among them, whose reads resolve against the reviewed task's own folder (added in 0.70). In a verify session about a task whose assignee's role has a private folder, the task's reviewer and the Product Manager, who accepts it, may read that folder, and read it in place of a folder of their own, whatever role they have; any other call is refused `sheet_refused`. Readiness holds the reviewer of such a task to the Product Manager (5.3), and the tool does not rely on it. With `baseline: true` it reads the workbook as the copy Catervas took of the folder when the session's task was assigned (`.history/<task-id>/<path>`, below), by the same path rules and the same limits, in a session about a task that works in the caller's folder; in a session about no task, or about a task that does not, it is refused `sheet_refused` (added in 0.62).

The three are offered as they may be called: to the Finance Specialist, `catervas_read_costs` in every session, and to each role with a private folder, `catervas_read_sheet` in every session and `catervas_write_sheet` in an implement session of a task; `catervas_read_costs` is the Finance Specialist's alone, so the Procurement Specialist is not offered it and is refused it `sheet_refused`, and, for `catervas_read_sheet`, to any verify session about a task of a role with a private folder.

Finance tasks, as built (added in 0.62; phase 7 step 09c). A task of the Finance Specialist's is a task in its private folder, found from the role by `private_folder(role)`, and runs there; every rule below keys on that one function, so another role's folder is one more arm of it (6.10). Its readiness is 5.3's `private_folder_task`, and its reviewer is held to the Product Manager alone by `private_folder_reviewer` (the founder's decision of 2026-10-07); its assignment waits while another task holds the folder (5.2), and then Catervas copies the folder's files but `.history/` to `.history/<task-id>/`, once, and starts the task there, with no worktree, branch or sandbox made. Its implement session, its reviewer's session and the Product Manager's acceptance session run with the folder as their working directory (made for its owner alone when it is not there) and with no executor, so the hook, which judges every path against the working directory (5.6, 8.2), lets them reach the folder and nothing outside it: not another task's worktree, not `.catervas/local/catervas.db`, not the files above the folder. The implement message names the role's folder, `.catervas/local/finance` for this role, and says that nothing there is committed (changed in 0.70, from "where your books are"). It asks for `verifying` with `catervas_request_transition`, whose `workbooks` names the workbooks it wrote, one to twenty paths in the folder (a list of none or of more is `InvalidInput`), and the governor's gate reads them from the folder (5.2); the files that changed since the copy are the task's diff, and the Definition of Done holds them to `allowed_paths` and excepts the folder from the protected paths (5.4). Catervas checks its `artifact` criteria on the host and shows its reviewer which files changed, each beside its copy (5.4). Once the Product Manager accepts it, it is finished with nothing to integrate and counts as integrated for the tasks that depend on it (5.14). Each task's copy, `.history/<task-id>/`, is kept, as every version of a workbook under `.history/` is (the tools' own copies, above), and is the restore point for a cancelled task, whose changes stay in the folder. Its prompt and its skill say all this: work in the private folder, nothing there is committed, record each `artifact` criterion with `catervas_record_criterion_result` before asking for `verifying`, and ask for it naming the `workbooks`. A session of its ends in one of three ways, as every role's does: it asks the human, declares itself blocked, or asks for `verifying`, and naming the `workbooks` is how the third goes.

Stripe, Digits and Kick (changed in 0.67; phase 7 step 10; ADR 0019's amendment of 2026-10-07). The role reads the services its kit offers (6.7), each connected by the user and each only read. Stripe, when the product uses it, gives the revenue, fees, payouts and refunds, through Stripe's official server: the user signs in to Stripe (route 1), choosing the account, live or a sandbox, and the permissions on Stripe's own page, and can end Catervas's access under ‘OAuth sessions’ in their Stripe user settings. Read-only is Catervas's own lock, which holds whatever the user ticks on Stripe's page: in the kit the tools that read the business's numbers are `network` and every tool that writes, whatever it writes, is `denied`, so the harness refuses a write before Stripe would. This replaces the restricted key with read permissions that 0.54 described, since a signed-in service refreshes its own access and a kit entry has one way in. Digits and Kick, which a business may already keep its books in, are signed in to and read the same way. The books carry totals and Stripe's own ids, never a customer's name, email or card: `stripe_api_read` takes any `GET`, so a charge comes back with its customer's details, Catervas cannot split one tool, and the role's prompt and the skill `using-finance-sources` hold the line. What that leaves open is a known risk (8.6).

Receipts intake (changed in 0.21 on the founder's decision of 2026-09-27, from a connector to the user's main mailbox; ships in the first release after the web release, phase 15 step 02). The user keeps a receipts mailbox of its own, an alias or a second address at their own provider, and forwards or routes receipts to it; vendors can bill it directly. Catervas reads that mailbox over IMAP, with an app password, or the provider's IMAP sign-in through Catervas Cloud where it suits (changed in 0.61, ADR 0044), kept in the OS keychain; it reads no other mailbox, so the boundary is the mailbox itself rather than a filter Catervas promises to apply, and no provider's restricted-scope verification applies. The connector asks for unread messages, has no call that changes the mailbox, never hands over a message already filed, accepts a sender only from the user's approved list, and gives the agent each message's text, and each attachment it files, under the untrusted-content notice (8.6). An attachment is filed only if it is a PDF or an image and at most 10 MB. The mailbox settings, the approved senders and the ledger of filed messages live in `.catervas/local/finance/mailbox.json` (8.4). The user drops no bill anywhere; a bank or card statement, the one file every bank exports as CSV, goes under `imports/` and is the source the books are reconciled against, because a receipt describes a purchase and a statement shows the money. `catervas finance connect`, `catervas finance disconnect` and the web app's finance setup manage the mailbox; `mailbox.connected` and `mailbox.disconnected` record it. A connector to the user's main mailbox through Google's or Microsoft's mail API, filtered by a label or folder, is not planned: it needs Google's restricted-scope verification with a paid yearly assessment, its filter is Catervas's promise rather than the provider's enforcement, and it is built only if users ask once receipts intake has shipped.

When it works.
