While a process drives the project, Catervas starts a receipts sweep once a day, as a tick rule like the standup (5.9): the connector fetches the new messages first, and a sweep with nothing new starts no session and costs nothing; otherwise a short Finance Specialist session files each receipt and records it in the books, its cost under the `finance` purpose (F17). `sweep.started` and `sweep.ended` are recorded either way, so the day's tick has a last run to count from. The user can start one with "Check now" or `catervas finance sweep`. `receipt.filed` records each receipt. One piece of finance work touches the folder at a time, and a sweep is one: once receipts intake ships, a sweep does not start while a task holds the folder (5.2), and a finance task is not assigned while a sweep runs. A finance task is otherwise a task in the folder, as "Finance tasks, as built" above says: its `allowed_paths` are under `.catervas/local/finance/`, its criteria are `artifact`, `review` and `human`, its session runs in the folder, it names its `workbooks` to reach `verifying`, its reviewer is shown what changed beside the copy taken at its assignment, and once `accepted` it is finished (5.14).

The mailbox plumbing (added in 0.77; phase 7 step 10f). The reading and sending of a mailbox of the user's own is Catervas's, in `runtime/src/mailbox.rs`: providers and their servers, the login checked over TLS with the platform's certificates, `MailboxSecrets` (the password kept like a connector's keys, one per purpose), the alias rule that opens only the mail Catervas expects, and the ledger that numbers a folder. The Procurement Specialist is its first user (6.10); the Finance Specialist's receipts intake (phase 15 step 02) reuses it and does not rewrite it, with its own purpose and its own folder.

### 6.7 Role kits (added in 0.22)

Each role ships a kit, `roles/<role>/kit.yaml`, validated against `kit.schema.json` (ADR 0020; `docs/design/role-kits.md`): the skills it carries, in the Agent Skills format of section 6, and the connectors it may be given, each an MCP server (5.6) with its setup copy for the wizard, the credential keys it needs, every tool tagged by the kit, and its tool list pinned so that a test fails when the service's list drifts, each pin update re-reviewing the tags. Every connector is optional; a role works without any. The user connects one by signing in or pasting a key from the page the copy names, never by tagging a tool, in the web app's connector screens or with `catervas connect <agent> <connector>` and `catervas disconnect <agent> <connector>`; `connector.connected` and `connector.disconnected` record it (8.5). Credentials are per agent. A kit's connector is connected this way from phase 7 step 05, and signing in from step 03; a server of the user's own is connected now, with pasted keys, as the last paragraph of this section says (added in 0.38). Kits are free (section 9).

A connector's tool is tagged `network`, a read-only remote call that runs; `external_effect`, a call that changes anything outside the sandbox or spends the user's credits, which asks; or `denied`, never offered to the agent. It is never `read`, which means the project's files: a remote read is an outbound call carrying arguments the agent chose. Connecting a connector gives the agent that server's tagged tools whatever the agent's own tiers; the tag governs the call.

A tool that spends the user's credits, generating or editing one image or one video clip, or that only drafts what the human sends from the service itself, such as an email draft in Kit, carries an allowance: the number of calls per sprint, or per UTC day when the project runs no sprints (5.5), that are pre-approved, per tool and per agent, set by the user when connecting from the kit's default. A call beyond the allowance asks the human as any `external_effect` does, and the human may raise it. A batch tool, and any tool that publishes, sends, posts or pays, has no allowance and always asks, unless the owner's approved marketing plan covers the act (6.5; ADR 0042; added in 0.52), as does one a kit judges too costly to pre-approve: a 3D model, a video edit priced by its length, an analysis, or a change to the account's library. A voice-over always asks as well (added in 0.51), because Higgsfield's `generate_audio` can copy a voice from a sample of audio and a kit cannot refuse one argument of a tool, so the human sees the whole input of every one. An image may use an identity the user trained in their own account, which no kit trains, so using it is the user's earlier choice. The count is a projection of the `tool.called` events the hook records for every call (8.5), and the board shows it against the allowance beside the model spend. An allowance counts calls, not money, because Catervas cannot price another service's credits. Everything a connector returns is untrusted content (8.6), and a message read from a chat bridge answers no question and approves nothing.

Allowances in the team file (added in 0.45; ADR 0037). A kit entry in `team.yaml` may hold `allowances: { <tool>: 0..1000 }`, per agent: `allowance_not_kit` on an entry that is not `source: kit`, `allowance_not_external` for a tool it does not tag `external_effect`, each at `/agents/<i>/mcp_servers/<j>/allowances/<tool>`. `spec_sha256` holds `allowances` only when it is not empty, so a commit that raises one makes the server "Connect again". At connect each starts at the kit's `calls`; `connector.connect` and `catervas connect --allowance <tool>=<n>` (kit connectors only, `kit_names_these` otherwise) set them, `allowance_not_offered` refuses a tool the kit gives none, `allowance_out_of_range` a number outside 0 to 1,000. `connector.allowances { agent, server, allowances }` changes them without the key; a tool left out keeps its number: the kept entry must be the team file's (`connector_not_confirmed`), and a service the kit has since changed is `connector_not_in_kit`. A change applies from the agent's next session and does not allow a call that waits. The period is the open sprint's, else the later of the UTC day's start and the last `sprint.ended`. The count is every `tool.called` of that agent, server and full tool name in the period, a call a grant allowed among them, so it may exceed the allowance (the board and Costs show "21 of 20 images" and say "Extra images were ones you approved."). The query `allowances.list {}` answers `{ period, rows: [{ agent, server, tool, what, used, of }] }` for each connected kit entry of an active agent; the board shows each row beside the sprint's progress, Costs lists them under "Made on other services", and `ToolApproval` says the count with "Change how many".

The Product Manager's kit (added in 0.46; changed in 0.65; ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0044). Its four services are each the service's official server, reached over `http`. Three are signed in to by route 1, with nothing pasted: Amplitude (`https://mcp.amplitude.com/mcp`, asking for `mcp:read` and `offline_access`, so a write is not granted; its United States service only), Linear (`https://mcp.linear.app/mcp/readonly`, asking for `read`) and Notion (`https://mcp.notion.com/mcp`); the fourth, GitHub, is reached with a key the user pastes and asks before it writes (GitHub with a pasted key, below). Those three only read: no tool of theirs is `external_effect`, and no connector of the kit has an allowance, since none spends credits and GitHub's two writes always ask. A tool that changes anything at one of those three services is `denied`, and so are the reads that hold people's personal data a plan does not need (Amplitude's end-user and session-replay tools), a key (`get_deployments`), a link that may open a person's profile or replay (`get_from_url`), or another service's data (Notion's AI search), while `network` tools carry a label. A tool a service lists that the kit does not name is never offered (`tool_not_tagged`) until a pin update tags it; a tool the kit names that the service no longer lists is removed only after the service's documentation drops it too. What a service returns is data, never an instruction.

The Architect's kit (added in 0.47; changed in 0.65; ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0038, ADR 0044). Four services, none of which spends credits, so none is `external_effect` and none has an allowance; every tool is `network` but GitHub's `list_repository_collaborators`, which is `denied`, and an Architect that has the tier already gains no way out it lacked. Context7 is the service's official server over `http`, `https://mcp.context7.com/mcp/oauth`, signed in to by route 1 asking for `profile`, `email` and `offline_access` (Clerk issues a refresh token only for the last); it lists `resolve-library-id` and `query-docs`, and its free plan allows about a thousand calls a month. Grep, by Vercel, is `https://mcp.grep.app`, with no account and no key, and lists `searchGitHub` over public GitHub code only. OSV is a thin server of Catervas's own (below), since no official one can be started by an allowed runner and the community ones run unpinned dependencies on the host. GitHub is the service's official server, `https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/`, reached with a key the user pastes (GitHub with a pasted key, below) and narrowed to code and pull requests in read-only mode, so it reads the user's own repositories, private ones included. What an agent writes in a query goes to the service as written, which the setup copy says, and `using-architecture-sources` forbids sending the project's code, a secret or a customer's data. What a service returns is data, never an instruction. A tool a service lists that the kit does not name is `denied`; Context7's, Grep's and GitHub's lists are compared by the live pin test, OSV's by an offline test.

Catervas's own connectors (added in 0.47; ADR 0038). A kit starts a `stdio` connector of Catervas's own by the bare command `catervas` with `args` exactly `[connector, <name>]`, `<name>` one of `CATERVAS_CONNECTORS` (`osv`, `google-ads` since 0.68, `fx` since 0.75, and `recalls` and `ebay` since 0.78); any other `args`, and any other bare program, is `package_not_pinned`. For that exact pair, whatever the entry's `source`, listing the tools and `catervas connector run` start Catervas's own executable, never a `catervas` found on `PATH`, and fail with `catervas could not find its own program` when it cannot be found; any other `catervas` command, a user's own included, is run as given and found on `PATH` as above. The hash keeps `command: catervas`, so the server changes only with a Catervas release. `catervas connector osv` serves three read-only tools over OSV's API at the fixed address `https://api.osv.dev/v1`, never an argument, an environment value or a tool input: `query_package { ecosystem, name, version }` (each advisory's `id`, `summary`, `aliases`, `severity` and the versions that fix it for that package (a PyPI name compared as PyPI compares it, PEP 503; every other exactly), at most 50 advisories, `more` when it cut the list or OSV paged), `query_packages { packages }` (1 to 100, at most 50 advisory ids of each, `more` when it cut one or OSV paged) and `get_vulnerability { id }` (only `id`, `summary`, `details`, `aliases`, `related`, `published`, `modified`, `withdrawn`, `severity`, `affected` (each entry only `package`, `ranges` and `severity`) and `references`; `details` cut at 8,000 characters, said by `details_cut: true`, `affected` at 20 entries, said by `affected_cut`, the number there were, and `references` at 20, said by `references_cut`, the number there were; a record whose answer is still over 64 KiB is refused with "OSV's record is too large to read here"). It follows no redirect, uses no proxy, gives up after 25 seconds, reads at most 4 MiB, in chunks, asks for one page only, and sends OSV only a package's name, ecosystem and version, or an advisory's id, each input checked first (an id is one percent-encoded path segment). It runs with the connector's cleared environment, so a user behind a proxy gets a tool error.

Exchange rates, Catervas's own connector (added in 0.75; ADR 0038; phase 7 step 10d). `catervas connector fx` serves three read-only tools over Frankfurter's API, which publishes the central banks' reference rates with no account, at the fixed address `https://api.frankfurter.dev/v2`, never an argument, an environment value or a tool input: `latest_rates { base, quotes }` (`{ base, rates: { <quote>: { rate, date } } }`, each quote with the day Frankfurter gave it, since the banks it reads publish on different days; a quote Frankfurter gives no rate for is a tool error), `rate_on { base, quote, date }` (`{ date, base, quote, rate }`, `date` the day Frankfurter answered for, which may be earlier than the day asked, so a rate is never claimed for a day that had none) and `list_currencies {}` (`[{ code, name }]`, at most 400). Each input is checked before a request leaves: a code is three capital letters; `quotes` are 1 to 30 distinct codes, none the base; `quote` is not the base; a `date` is written `YYYY-MM-DD` exactly and is from 1999-01-04, the earliest day Catervas allows (Frankfurter answers some earlier days, the dirham on 1997-06-02 among them, and Catervas does not ask for them; changed in 0.75 after review), to today in UTC. It follows no redirect, uses no proxy, gives up after 15 seconds, reads at most 1 MiB, in chunks, builds its query from pairs and never by formatting it into the address, and sends Frankfurter only currency codes and a day. Whatever Frankfurter answers that is not rates, a redirect and an unknown code (422) included, is the one sentence "Frankfurter could not answer that; check the codes and the date", without its body, and an answer over 1 MiB is "Frankfurter's answer is too large". It runs with the connector's cleared environment, so a user behind a proxy gets a tool error. Its three tools are `network` in the Procurement Specialist's kit, and its pin is the offline test `fx_server_lists_the_kits_tools`, as OSV's is.

Safety recalls, Catervas's own connector (added in 0.78; ADR 0038; phase 7 step 10g). `catervas connector recalls` serves five read-only tools over the United States' product-safety and vehicle-safety agencies' public data, with no account, at three fixed addresses, never an argument, an environment value or a tool input: the Consumer Product Safety Commission's `https://www.saferproducts.gov/RestWebServices`, NHTSA's `https://api.nhtsa.gov` and NHTSA's VIN decoder, vPIC, at `https://vpic.nhtsa.dot.gov/api`. `product_recalls { words, field, since? }` searches the CPSC's recalls for `words` (1 to 100 characters) in one field, `title`, `product_name` or `product_type`, which the CPSC takes as `RecallTitle`, `ProductName` and `ProductType` (never `Title`, which it ignores while answering all it has), from the day `since` (an ISO date) on, and answers the newest 50 by date as `{ recalls, more }`, each `{ number, date, title, url, description, products, hazards, remedies, manufacturers, retailers, countries }`, a description over 2,000 characters cut and said by `description_cut: true`; its search is a plain text match, so the skill searches a product's name, then its type, then its maker's. `vehicle_recalls`, `vehicle_complaints` and `vehicle_safety_ratings`, each `{ make, model, model_year }`, answer NHTSA's: `{ recalls }` (each `{ campaign, date, component, summary, consequence, remedy, park_it, park_outside }`), `{ count, by_component, newest }` (a component counted once for a complaint, the 20 newest by the day filed, read as MM/DD/YYYY, each summary cut at 600 characters and said by `summary_cut: true`) and `{ ratings, more }` (each `{ vehicle, overall, frontal, side, rollover }`, for at most 10 versions of the car, an id that is not a positive whole number skipped). `decode_vin { vin }` answers the twelve fields of vPIC's decoding that name the car (`Make`, `Model`, `ModelYear`, `Trim`, `BodyClass`, `EngineCylinders`, `DisplacementL`, `FuelTypePrimary`, `DriveType`, `PlantCountry`, `ErrorCode`, `ErrorText`). Each input is checked before a request leaves: a make or a model is 1 to 40 ASCII letters, digits, spaces and hyphens, so it is one path segment; `model_year` is a whole number from 1950 to next year; a VIN is 17 capital letters and digits, none of them I, O or Q; `since` is a real day written `YYYY-MM-DD`. It follows no redirect, uses no proxy, gives up after 20 seconds, reads at most 4 MiB, in chunks (a vehicle's complaints 8 MiB, since a popular car's are several megabytes), builds its queries from pairs and its paths from segments, never by formatting them into an address, never asks for an address an answer carries, and sends the agencies only the agent's words, a make, a model, a year or a VIN. Whatever an agency answers that is not rows, a redirect included, is "The CPSC could not answer that" or "NHTSA could not answer that" (vPIC is NHTSA's), without its body, and an answer over the cap is "The CPSC's answer is too large; ask a narrower question" or "NHTSA's answer is too large to read here". It runs with the connector's cleared environment, so a user behind a proxy gets a tool error. It covers products and vehicles sold in the United States only; the EU's and Canada's lists are candidates a data pipeline may bring. Its five tools are `network` in the Procurement Specialist's kit, its pin is the offline test `recalls_server_lists_the_kits_tools`, and `live_catervas_servers_answer` calls each tool once at the real hosts, by hand.

eBay listings, Catervas's own connector (added in 0.78; ADR 0038, ADR 0039's amendment of 2026-10-07, ADR 0043; phase 7 step 10g). `catervas connector ebay` serves two read-only tools over eBay's official Browse API at the fixed address `https://api.ebay.com`, never an argument, an environment value or a tool input, with the user's own free eBay developer keys (`credential_keys` `EBAY_CLIENT_ID` and `EBAY_CLIENT_SECRET`, from `https://developer.ebay.com/my/keys`), which reach it only through the launcher's cleared environment (ADR 0030) and are held as secrets, never in an answer, an error or a log. With either key missing or empty it still lists both tools, and every call is the tool error "eBay is not set up; connect it again with your App ID and Cert ID" with nothing sent. At its first call it asks `POST /identity/v1/oauth2/token` for an application grant (`Authorization: Basic` of the two keys, `grant_type=client_credentials` and the scope `https://api.ebay.com/oauth/api_scope`) and keeps the token in memory, asking again when less than a minute of it remains (eBay's lasts 7,200 seconds and is believed for a day at most; two calls at once make one grant); the Basic header goes with the grant alone, and a search or an item request carries `Authorization: Bearer` of the token and nothing else. A search or an item that eBay refuses with 401 drops the token, so the next call asks for a new grant (changed in 0.78 after review). A grant refused (400, 401 or 403) is "eBay refused the App ID and Cert ID; connect it again with your production keys"; a 429 from either request is "eBay's limit for these keys is used up for now; try again later"; a 404 for an item is "eBay has no listing with that id; it may have ended"; any other refusal, a redirect included, is "eBay could not answer that", never with eBay's body. `search_items { words, marketplace, condition?, min_price?, max_price?, limit? }` searches fixed-price (Buy It Now) listings, asking prices and never bids, in `EBAY_US`, `EBAY_GB`, `EBAY_DE`, `EBAY_AU`, `EBAY_CA`, `EBAY_FR`, `EBAY_IT` or `EBAY_ES` (sent as `X-EBAY-C-MARKETPLACE-ID`), for `words` of 1 to 100 characters, `new` or `used`, a price from and to as a decimal text of up to 7 digits and 2 after the point (`min_price` not above `max_price`, with the marketplace's currency in the filter `price:[10..50],priceCurrency:GBP`) and 1 to 50 listings, 20 when left out, answering `{ items, total, more }`, each item `{ item_id, title, price, currency, condition, seller_feedback_score, seller_feedback_percent, location: { country, postal_code }, shipping?, url }`. `get_item { item_id }`, an id from a search (`v1|` and 6 to 20 digits, `|`, 1 to 20 digits), answers the same fields and the description as plain text (cut at 4,000 characters and said by `description_cut: true`), `return_terms` and at most 30 `item_specifics`. No answer carries a seller's username, which eBay's API License Agreement forbids collecting: only the seller's feedback score and percentage and the listing's address, which Catervas never asks for. A listing's title and description are the seller's words, untrusted (8.6). It follows no redirect, uses no proxy, gives up after 20 seconds, reads at most 4 MiB, in chunks, builds its query from pairs and its path from segments, and sends eBay only the agent's words and filters. Its two tools are `network` in the Procurement Specialist's kit, its pin is the offline test `ebay_server_lists_the_kits_tools` (with two obviously fake keys, and another without either), and `live_catervas_servers_answer` calls each tool once at eBay with the founder's keys in `CATERVAS_KIT_EBAY_EBAY_CLIENT_ID` and `CATERVAS_KIT_EBAY_EBAY_CLIENT_SECRET`.

The built-in connector (added in 0.33; ADR 0026). Before any kit, Catervas ships one connector of its own, Playwright (5.6), held to the same rules: the `container` connector `playwright` of the UI/UX Designer's kit, `roles/ui_ux_designer/kit.yaml` (changed in 0.44, from `crates/roles/connectors/playwright.yaml`), pins its image by digest (`@playwright/mcp` 0.0.82 on 2026-09-30), names the folder its Playwright is kept in, and tags every tool the image lists, the browsing tools `network` and every other `denied`. A test in Docker lists the pinned image's tools against the kit, and a tool a new pin adds goes in as `denied` in the same change, until a plan tags it ("add each as denied in roles/ui_ux_designer/kit.yaml"). `builtin_connector(name)` answers what the kit holds; the team file still names it `{ name: playwright, source: builtin }`.

The Marketing Specialist's kit (added in 0.51; ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0037, ADR 0040). Two creative services, each the service's official server over `http` and signed in to by route 1, with nothing pasted, and each spending the user's credits; the posting and email services follow in the next paragraph. Higgsfield (`https://mcp.higgsfield.ai/mcp`, asking for `openid`, `email` and `offline_access`) needs a paid plan, and every generation through an agent uses credits whatever the plan, which its setup copy says. Recraft (`https://mcp.recraft.ai/mcp`, asking for `openid`, `email` and `profile`) has no `offline_access` scope and no revocation address, so its grant may lapse when it expires and the agent then shows "Sign in again", which its setup copy says too. A tool that makes one image, clip or edit is `external_effect` with an allowance: Higgsfield's `generate_image` 20 "images", `generate_video` 6 "video requests", `upscale_image` 10 "image upscales", `remove_background` 10 "background removals" and `outpaint_image` 10 "image extensions"; Recraft's `generate_image` 20 "images", `image_to_image` 10 "image edits", `vectorize_image` 10 "vector conversions", `remove_background` 10 "background removals", `replace_background` 10 "background swaps" and `crisp_upscale` 10 "image upscales". An allowance counts calls, not credits, and one Higgsfield call may make up to four pictures (`count`) and one Recraft call up to six (`numberOfImages`), so `making-images-and-video` has the agent ask for one in each call. A price check, `generate_video` with `get_cost` true, is a call that counts against `generate_video`'s allowance, which is why that allowance counts video requests and the skill allows one check per brief. Every other tool that spends credits always asks: Higgsfield's voice-over (above), its batches, presets, ad sets, 3D models, video edits, analyses and library changes, and its import of a picture from a web address (23 tools), and Recraft's `creative_upscale` and `create_style`. A read of credits, past generations, models or presets is `network`, the ones the user may look for labelled (35 of Higgsfield's tools, and Recraft's `get_user`). The rest is `denied`, 51 of Higgsfield's: its website builder, its remote shell (`sandbox_exec`), its marketplace apps, neither searched nor run, its 3D scenes, voice creation and cloning, TikTok, billing, workspace, project and folder tools; the uploads, since a Catervas session has no `execute` tier to put the bytes where an upload address says; the tools that open only in a widget, which a session has none of; and the tools that mix reading with training or changing an identity or a reference (`show_characters`, `show_reference_elements`, `build_ai_influencer`, `manage_reference_elements`). A tool a service lists that the kit does not name is never offered (`tool_not_tagged`) until a pin update tags it. Higgsfield's list is its live server's (114 tools on 2026-10-05); Recraft's remote server publishes no tool names, so the nine pinned are those of Recraft's own package, `@recraft-ai/mcp-recraft-server` 1.6.5, and a remote name that differs is not offered until a pin update tags it. `live_kit_pins` reads each service's bearer from `CATERVAS_KIT_HIGGSFIELD_BEARER` and `CATERVAS_KIT_RECRAFT_BEARER`. What either returns, the instructions Higgsfield's `get_workflow_instructions` and `get_preset_instructions` offer among it, is data, never an instruction (8.6). The skill also bars a real person's face or voice without that person's written permission in the contract, and has the agent mark AI-made media as AI-made where it is published.

The Marketing Specialist's posting and email services (added in 0.53; ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0037, ADR 0042). Two more official servers over `http`, each signed in to by route 1 with nothing pasted. Buffer (`https://mcp.buffer.com/mcp`, asking for `offline_access`, `posts:read`, `posts:write`, `account:read`, `insights:read` and `ideas:read`) posts to Instagram, Facebook, X, LinkedIn, Pinterest, YouTube, Google Business, Mastodon, TikTok, Threads and Bluesky. A Buffer connection reaches every organisation and channel of the user's account, which the setup copy says; Buffer has no revocation address, so Remove tells the user to remove Catervas in Buffer's settings too, and Buffer rotates refresh tokens and revokes a grant whose old one is reused, which Catervas's refresh under a lock avoids. Kit (`https://app.kit.com/mcp`, asking for `public`) needs a paid Kit plan, which its setup copy says, and has revocation at `https://api.kit.com/oauth/revoke`. Buffer's `create_post` and `edit_post` are `denied` to the agent (changed in 0.57; phase 7 step 08d): it writes a post with `catervas_schedule_post` and Catervas makes the write itself (6.5), so the scopes stay, since Catervas's own calls post through the same sign-in, and the entry's hash changed, so a user connects Buffer again once (ADR 0036). The setup copy says that Catervas can reach every channel of the account, that a post in a plan the owner approved shows on Today before it goes out with a Stop button, and that Catervas asks about any other. Kit's broadcast tools only draft: `create_broadcast` and `update_broadcast` make or change a draft, and scheduling and sending stay in Kit's own editor, through the `confirm_url` the draft returns and the completion note carries, so nothing reaches a subscriber until the human sends it. They are `external_effect` with an allowance of 10 "email drafts" each, rather than asking for each draft and again in Kit. Kit's sequence, sequence-email, landing-page and snippet writes change what a subscriber or the public sees (a sequence email is live once it is `published`, which the skill forbids and a hook cannot see), so each is `external_effect` with no allowance and always asks, as do `list_forms` and `get_landing_page`, reads that Kit marks as writes because resolving a public address can queue a rebuild of saved content. A read of posts, channels, campaigns or their numbers is `network` (10 of Buffer's 20 tools and 31 of Kit's 83). The rest is `denied`: every deletion, since a post or an email cannot be taken back; Buffer's idea and template writes and its generic `introspect_schema`, `execute_query` and `execute_mutation`, which reach the whole API and so cannot be tagged, and, since 0.57, `create_post` and `edit_post` (10 of Buffer's); and, in Kit (40 of its 83), every write to subscribers, tags, custom fields, products, colours or webhooks, `unsubscribe` among them, every bulk tool, `list_tax_codes` and `list_webhooks`, and every read of a subscriber's or a buyer's own data, for the reason the Product Manager's kit denies Amplitude's end-user tools: a campaign needs totals, not people. A tool a service lists that the kit does not name is never offered (`tool_not_tagged`) until a pin update tags it. Buffer's twenty tools are those of its published guide to its server and Kit's 83 those of its help centre's list of its connector's tools (both read 2026-10-05); `live_kit_pins` reads their bearers from `CATERVAS_KIT_BUFFER_BEARER` and `CATERVAS_KIT_KIT_BEARER`. The role's words change with them: its prompt, its `forbidden` list and its skills no longer say it never publishes. Since step 08c (changed in 0.55) its `forbidden` list says it publishes, sends or spends money except through a call the owner allows or the owner's approved marketing plan (6.5), and since step 08d (changed in 0.57) its skills say that a social post in the owner's approved marketing plan goes out through `catervas_schedule_post`, that any other post waits for the owner, that an email is a draft the human sends, and that otherwise the human publishes; the plan's ads are Google Ads' (added in 0.68). The ninth skill, `posting-and-email`, is the email skill since 0.57 (its posting rules went to `running-social-channels`, the kit's fourteenth skill, which has the agent read `list_channels`, fill the active plan's slots with `catervas_schedule_post` at least three hours ahead and on the slot's day in the business's own offset, with `details` for YouTube and Pinterest, use only the business's own pictures, ones the owner gave it or ones it made, and read results with `get_aggregated_post_metrics` before the next plan). It has the agent hand over a broadcast's `confirm_url`, keep a series' emails unpublished, keep each email or page under the 64 KiB input limit, a longer one becoming two emails in a series or a shorter page, since Kit's updates replace the whole body (`tool_input_too_large`), and never put a subscriber's details in a prompt or a note. Search-engine data (Semrush) is not in the kit: it publishes no tool names and its access goes in the request body, which Catervas's header cannot set; per-network servers are not in it either, since X's own server cannot post and charges per post, Meta admits only listed clients, and LinkedIn, TikTok and YouTube have none.

The Finance Specialist's kit (added in 0.67; ADR 0019's amendment of 2026-10-07, ADR 0035, ADR 0036). Six skills, `categorising-expenses`, `closing-the-month`, `forecasting`, `unit-economics-and-pricing`, `recommending-a-budget` and `using-finance-sources`, and three services, each the service's official server over `http` and signed in to by route 1, with nothing pasted. Stripe (`https://mcp.stripe.com`) asks for the one scope `mcp`, pinned because with none named Catervas would ask for every scope the server advertises, a wider one added later among them; Digits (`https://api.digits.com/mcp`) names none; Kick (`https://use.kick.co/mcp`) asks for `mcp:read` alone, which Kick documents as the scope for any call that only looks up data, so a write is refused at Kick as well as here. Digits and Kick are bookkeeping services a business may already keep its books in, which the role reads in place of rebuilding them, and are not Kit, the Marketing Specialist's email service; each is the business's own account, and Catervas adds nothing to its cost. The role only reads: seven of Stripe's ten tools, nine of Digits' ten and seventeen of Kick's 39 are `network`, each with a label, and the rest are `denied`, which is every tool that writes whatever it writes (`stripe_api_write`, Kick's `*_act`, `*_create` and `*_update` tools and `activity_undo`), `send_stripe_feedback`, which sends something to Stripe's company, `stripe_implementation_planner`, which builds an integration and reads no numbers, `list_business_users`, the people with access to the books, which the role does not need, and Kick's `list_kick_skills` and `load_kick_skill`, which load Kick's own instructions into the session, where the role's instructions are Catervas's skills and a service's words are data (8.6). No tool is `external_effect`, so no service has an allowance. A service lists the tools its grant allows (Kick: “Available Kick tools can vary by token scope”), so a pinned tool a grant does not list costs nothing (ADR 0036), and the live pin test lists each service with a bearer from a sign-in granted widely, which the kit never asks for. The skills' rules: a category is one line of the books' `Categories` sheet, and a new one has a reason in its row's note, with no tax category given as advice; a month is closed only when every difference between Stripe, the ledger, Catervas's costs and the books of more than one per cent is explained in a row's note with both figures, by a `Status` cell on the month's `Monthly summary` row reading `closed`; a forecast has a method named, a range, its assumptions on a sheet of their own, and its date and inputs; a price change and a budget are recommendations the user decides, a daily limit in Settings and a sprint's when the user starts it (5.5); and every figure from a service names its source and its dates. Stripe's tools are those of its documentation, Digits' of its help page for its server and Kick's 39 of its tool reference (each read 2026-10-07); `live_kit_pins` reads their bearers from `CATERVAS_KIT_STRIPE_BEARER`, `CATERVAS_KIT_DIGITS_BEARER` and `CATERVAS_KIT_KICK_BEARER`. QuickBooks Online is not in the kit: whether its registration accepts Catervas is for a later step to probe, and Xero's server registers no client by itself, so it waits for Catervas Cloud.

The Procurement Specialist's kit (added in 0.75; ADR 0039 and its amendments of 2026-10-07, ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0038, ADR 0043; phase 7 step 10d). Fourteen skills (twelve when it was added in 0.75; `requesting-a-data-pipeline` since 0.76 and `contacting-sellers` since 0.77, in 6.10), `defining-the-need`, `finding-sellers-and-makers`, `comparing-offers`, `reading-terms-and-pricing`, `checking-a-seller`, `checking-product-safety`, `estimating-landed-cost`, `checking-a-used-vehicle`, `keeping-the-vendor-register`, `reviewing-renewals`, `writing-purchase-orders`, `using-procurement-sources`, `requesting-a-data-pipeline` and `contacting-sellers`, each written for any product, and seven services (five when it was added in 0.75; `recalls` and `ebay` since 0.78), in this order, none of which can buy, check out, sign or send. `fx`, `recalls` and `ebay` are Catervas's own (above). Exa (`https://mcp.exa.ai/mcp`, its official server, with no key and no sign-in) is searched and never asked for a page: `web_search_exa` is `network`, and `web_fetch_exa` is `denied`, because Exa fetches on its own servers and follows a redirect to another site, which the approved-sites check cannot see (8.6). SerpApi (`https://mcp.serpapi.com/mcp`, its official server) takes the user's own key, pasted and sent as a bearer header; its `search` takes any engine's parameters, the shops' engines being `google_shopping`, `amazon`, `ebay` and `walmart` (the skill uses `google_shopping`, `amazon` and `walmart` and reads eBay only through `ebay`, 6.10, changed in 0.78; the lens and reverse-image engines, `google_lens`, `yandex_images`, `google_reverse_image`, `bing_reverse_image` and `google_ai_mode`, make SerpApi fetch an address on its own servers, which its setup says in plain words and 8.6 records; added in 0.75 after review), and spends one of the user's searches, so it is `external_effect` with an allowance of 50 calls, "shopping searches", and a search past the allowance asks the human before it reaches SerpApi; `search_table` and `search_dashboard` are `denied`, being on-screen versions of `search` that take the same parameters and spend searches too. Brex (`https://api.brex.com/mcp`, its official server) is signed in to by route 1, asking for `offline_access`, `vendors.readonly`, `expenses.card.readonly` and `departments.readonly`, so a write is refused at Brex as well as here (its bills scope, `expenses.bill`, also writes and is not asked): eleven of its 43 tools are `network` (vendors, bills, merchants and their categories, expense categories and analytics, card charges and departments), each with a label, and thirty-two are `denied`, every one that writes, the people tools, which carry card holders and limits, and the card, limit, bank, reward, travel, accounting and set-up tools; before anyone signs in, an account admin or card admin in Brex accepts its Developer API agreement. AWS Pricing (`uvx awslabs.aws-pricing-mcp-server==1.1.1`, AWS Labs' official server, which needs the program `uv`) takes the user's `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, of a user allowed only the five pricing reads: six of its nine tools are `network`, and `analyze_cdk_project`, `analyze_terraform_project` and `generate_cost_report` are `denied`, since they read or write a path the agent names on the user's computer, where a server run on the host runs. Its first run downloads Python and the package and may outlast the 30-second listing limit, so the user connects again, and the second run is served from `uv`'s cache. Every service is optional, and the kit carries no credential of Catervas's (ADR 0043). The skills' rules: the rule that the agent never places, pays for, confirms or cancels an order is said in `writing-purchase-orders` alone, where an order is written; a skill that sends the agent to a site Catervas's list may lack says to ask for it with `catervas_request_sites` or to say plainly what could not be checked; the agent never recommends a product with an open recall and never says a car is sound; with "Safety recalls" connected it searches a product's recalls by name, then by type, then by its maker's name, and reads a used car's VIN by `decode_vin`, then `vehicle_recalls`, `vehicle_complaints` and `vehicle_safety_ratings`, keeping the request for the agencies' own pages as the fallback (changed in 0.78); from Brex it reports vendors and amounts, never people; it never sets Exa's `category` to `people`; and without `fx` it keeps each price in the seller's currency and converts nothing by guess. Exa's and SerpApi's tools are from their keyless `tools/list`, AWS Pricing's from its package's registrations (read 2026-10-08) and Brex's from its documentation (2026-10-05 and 2026-10-07); `live_kit_pins` reads Brex's bearer, an API token which its server accepts, from `CATERVAS_KIT_BREX_BEARER`, SerpApi's key from `CATERVAS_KIT_SERPAPI_SERPAPI_KEY` and AWS's from `CATERVAS_KIT_AWS_PRICING_AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `CATERVAS_KIT_AWS_PRICING_AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, and `fx`, `recalls` and `ebay` are pinned offline, the last two with `live_catervas_servers_answer` called by hand.

Catervas calls a connector's tool itself (added in 0.57; phase 7 step 08d, ADR 0042's amendment). `connectors::call_tool` makes one call of one tool of a server, beside `list_tools`, with the same start (a `stdio` server in its folder with only its keys and a few safe variables, an `http` one with its filled headers and bearer): one MCP session for the call, opened, `tools/call`, cancelled, within 30 seconds, past which it is `ConnectorError::Timeout`. It answers the result's structured content when there is one, else its first text block read as JSON, else `{ "text": <text> }`; a result marked as an error, and a JSON-RPC error to the call, are `ConnectorError::ToolError` with the text cut at 500 characters, which Catervas passes on only as untrusted words. Only a fixed list of Catervas's own calls is ever made, `OWN_CALLS` (`daemon/own_calls.rs`): Buffer's `get_channel`, `create_post` and `delete_post`; any other pair is refused before anything starts, and a later step that has Catervas call a service itself adds its pairs to the list and calls through `call_as`, never beside it. `call_as(state, agent, server, tool, arguments)` takes the named agent's kit entry from the team file, requires that the entry still matches its role's kit and that the connection is kept (`NotConnected` otherwise), runs it in the connector folder with Catervas's own program, and refreshes a signed-in entry first as a session's start does, within 60 seconds of its expiring, falling back to a token still good (a lapse is `SignInAgain`); an entry with keys is used as kept. Its errors are `NotListed`, `NotConnected`, `SignInAgain`, `Failed`, `Timeout` and `Tool`. A call of Catervas's is not the agent's: it passes no hook, may name a tool the kit tags `denied` (Buffer's `delete_post`), and is recorded as the `social_post.*` events (6.5), so it charges none of the agent's tool-call limit for an act that is the owner's; it uses the connection of the agent whose post it is, and a post of an agent whose Buffer entry is gone, lapsed or changed fails at hand-over, saying so.

A user's own connectors (added in 0.38; ADR 0030). Before any kit, the user can give one agent any MCP server: a program Catervas starts on this computer (`stdio`) or a web address spoken to over streamable HTTP (`http`; the older SSE transport is not accepted). Its entry in the agent's `mcp_servers` is `{ name, source: custom, transport, command, args, url, headers, credential_keys, tools }`, checked by `validate_team`, each refusal at its own field: `transport` is required with `source: custom` and absent with `builtin`, which takes none of these fields; `command` and `args` go with `stdio` alone and `command` is required there, a bare name found on `PATH` or a full path, so a `command` holding a `/` that does not start with one, such as `./server` or `bin/mcp`, is refused (`command_not_absolute`), `url` and `headers` with `http` alone and `url` is required there; `catervas` and a built-in's name are `connector_name_reserved`; a `url` starts `http://` or `https://` and holds no userinfo and no query (`url_holds_secret`), because it is written to the committed team file as it is, so a service whose address holds its key cannot be added yet; `headers` (at most 16) are templates that may hold `{KEY}` for a key in `credential_keys` and nothing else (`header_key_unknown`), and a header with a word, split at `-` and `_` in any case, of `authorization`, `cookie`, `key`, `apikey`, `token`, `secret`, `auth`, `password`, `passwd`, `pass`, `credential` or `credentials` in its name must hold one and no value beside it: split at spaces and `;`, each piece is a `{KEY}`, a `name={KEY}`, or, first, a scheme of a letter then letters and digits such as `Bearer` or `OAuth2` (`header_holds_secret`; beside a placeholder, and `Cookie`, added in 0.38 after review), so no secret is in the file; an argument is refused at its place (`arg_holds_secret`, added in 0.38 after review) when it is the value given to a flag that names a key, as `--api-key abc`, `--token=abc` or `--api-key -abc`, the value not a number and not a flag starting `--`; when it is `NAME=value` with a name that names a key, as `API_KEY=abc`; when it is a credential header with its value, as `mcp-remote` takes after `--header`; or when it is shaped like a well-known service's key (`sk-`, `sk_live_`, `sk_test_`, `rk_live_`, `rk_test_`, `ghp_`, `gho_`, `ghs_`, `github_pat_`, `glpat-`, `xoxb-`, `xoxp-`, or `AKIA` and 16 capitals and digits). A name names a key when its last word at `-` and `_` is `token`, `password`, `passwd`, `pass`, `secret`, `auth`, `pat`, `apikey`, `credential` or `credentials`, or its last two are `api key`, `access key` or `secret key`, and its first is not `no`: `--key`, `--sort-key`, `--primary-key`, `--max-token 4096` and `--no-auth` are settings, its value never quoted, since a server's key goes in `credential_keys` and reaches it in its environment; `credential_keys` are at most 8 names matching `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,63}$`; and `tools` maps each tool to `network`, `external_effect` or `denied`. Connecting lists the server's tools: Catervas starts it, or reaches it, with the agent's keys, through the launcher's clean environment (8.2), and gives up after thirty seconds. A tool whose name falls outside `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`, which Claude Code would rewrite, is shown as one Catervas can't use and is not written, so a call to it is `tool_not_tagged`. The user labels each other tool; one left unlabelled is written `external_effect`. The tools written at connect are the server's pin: a tool it lists later is `tool_not_tagged` until the user connects it again. The agent's keys are its own, never another agent's: Catervas keeps them in the OS keychain, service `catervas`, account `connector:<project_id>:<agent_id>:<server>`, where `<project_id>` is the project's id on this machine: 32 random hex digits made the first time it is needed and kept in the user's Catervas state folder at `projects/<sha256 of the project's canonical path>` (the folder 0700, the file 0600, refused unless it holds 32 hex digits), never in the project, and not the log's `project_id`, which is the folder's name, so two projects in folders of one name, a clone into a folder of the same name, or a copy of the project's folder never find each other's keys (changed in 0.38 after review). A project moved to another path is a new project here, and its connectors are connected again. Keys are kept as one JSON object `{ spec_sha256, keys }` holding only the keys the entry names, and a server with no keys still gets one. On a computer with no keychain they go to `connectors.json` in the user's Catervas state folder under the same account, the folder 0700 and the file 0600, written beside and renamed over while a lock file, `connectors.json.lock`, holds off any other process; Catervas reads both stores, so an entry kept in the file before a keychain appeared is still found. With neither, connecting is refused `no_secret_store`. `spec_sha256` is the sha256 of the canonical JSON (compact, object keys sorted at every depth) of the entry's `transport`, `command`, `args`, `url`, `headers`, `credential_keys` and `tools`; the name is not in it, being part of where the keys are kept. What was connected on this machine is what runs: an entry the team file changed since, by a clone, a pulled branch, a template or a hand edit, or one never connected here, is unconfirmed, and runs nothing and is sent no key until the user connects it again (8.2), so a commit can neither move a server's address nor relabel a `denied` tool `network`. A `stdio` server runs on the host as the user, with the user's rights, in sandbox mode too, and always in a folder Catervas keeps for it outside the repository, `connectors/<project id>/<agent>/<server>` in the user's Catervas state folder, removed and made again, empty and 0700, each time its tools are listed and each time a session starts it (changed in 0.38 after review). When that state folder is inside the project, as when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` points into it, no stdio server is listed or started, and the refusal `state_inside_project` says so on the page and at the command line (added in 0.38 after review). Connecting a server again while a session runs it removes and remakes the folder it runs in, so that server's writes into its folder fail until it next starts; this fails closed, and Catervas runs one session at a time. Never in a task's worktree, which agents write to, nor anywhere in the repository: git checks out a file committed there on a clone or a pull, and `npx`, `uvx` and `uv run` look upward to the repository's root, so a planted `node_modules/.bin`, `.npmrc`, module, `pyproject.toml` or script would run on the host with the agent's keys. With no state folder (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `HOME` and `APPDATA` all unset), no stdio server is listed or started. Outside the repository is all the folder promises: a command on `PATH` that itself reads a project, say one given the repository's path as an argument, is the user's own choice. A relative path in `args` resolves in that folder, so a script of the user's is given by its full path. The program is the one the user named: a bare name is found on `PATH`. On the command line (unix), `catervas connect <agent> <name> (--command <program> [--arg <a>]... | --url <url> [--header '<Name>: <template>']...) [--key <NAME>]... [--tag <tool>=network|external_effect|denied]...` checks the entry against the team, reads each key's value from a line of standard input, without echo at a terminal, never from an argument, and refuses `--key NAME=value` without printing the value. It lists the tools in its own process, prints each with its tag, or that Catervas can't use it, labels a tool no `--tag` names `external_effect`, refuses a `--tag` for a tool the server did not list or one Catervas can't use (`tag_unknown_tool`, naming the tools it lists), so a misspelling never leaves the tool meant `external_effect` unsaid, and keeps the keys in its own process, so a key never crosses a socket. It then writes the team file and records `connector.connected` here, holding the run lock, when nothing drives the project, or else sends the command `connector_connect { agent, server, spec_sha256 }`, `server` the entry with no value in it; and it ends with "Kept in your computer's keychain" or "Kept in a private file only you can read". `--json` prints `{ tools, stored_in, said }`. `connector_connect` is refused when the entry breaks the team's rules, is not a custom server (`connector_not_custom`), or does not hash to `spec_sha256` (`connector_not_confirmed`). `catervas disconnect <agent> <name>` sends `connector_disconnect { agent, server }` first, which removes the entry and records `connector.disconnected` and is not found unless the agent has a custom server of that name, so a refused one deletes nothing; it then deletes that agent's keys from both stores and touches no other agent's. Keys are kept before the command runs, so a command refused after its check leaves keys with no entry, which run nothing and are replaced by the next connect. Retiring an agent, or removing it from the team by a save or a saved team applied, deletes its keys for each of its custom servers, and no other agent's; a save that takes one custom server from an agent deletes that agent's keys for it (added in 0.38 after review). A server that fails to answer, or to list its tools, is said to have failed in Catervas's words alone: its own error text, which may quote what it was sent, is not repeated. The web app does the same through three RPCs (F9).
Signing in to a service (added in 0.41; ADR 0033). An `http` server, or Catervas's own connector (a `stdio` entry that is exactly `catervas connector <name>`, ADR 0038; amended in 0.59), may be signed in to instead of given keys: its entry then holds `oauth: { client_id?, callback_port?, scopes? }`, and the presence of `oauth` means "signed in". `client_id` is a public client the service's app registration gave Catervas, at most 200 characters; `callback_port`, from 1024 to 65535, goes with `client_id` alone (`callback_port_without_client`); `scopes` are at most 16, each of RFC 6749's scope characters, so none holds a space or a quote. `validate_team` refuses `oauth` on a `stdio` server (`oauth_on_stdio`) unless it is exactly Catervas's own connector's pair, the command `catervas` and the arguments `connector` and a word matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$` (the kit loader holds the word to Catervas's own connectors), on which `client_id` and `callback_port` are refused (`catervas_connector_client`: the app's id and port are Catervas's); it refuses `oauth` beside `credential_keys` (`oauth_with_keys`) and beside a header named `Authorization` in any case (`oauth_header_conflict`), each at its field. `spec_sha256` holds `"oauth": { client_id, callback_port, scopes }` only when `oauth` is present, so every entry connected before keeps its hash, and a change to one of the three makes the server "Connect again". Catervas runs the sign-in, in the daemon for the web app and in `catervas connect`'s own process for the command line, through `rmcp`'s OAuth client with Catervas's own checks around it, because Claude Code's `-p` cannot sign in and keeps its tokens by its config folder, not by agent. It asks the server to initialize without a token and follows the `resource_metadata` of its 401's `WWW-Authenticate` (RFC 9728) and nothing else: a server that does not point to its metadata is `sign_in_not_offered`, and no endpoint is guessed. The authorization server must say it does PKCE with `S256` (`pkce_not_supported` otherwise, before anything is registered), name an `issuer`, and offer either a `registration_endpoint` or have the team file's `client_id` (`sign_in_not_supported` otherwise); every URL it names, the server's own, and every redirect Catervas is sent along must be `https`, or `http` on this computer's loopback, which the tests need, or the sign-in fails naming it. Registration, in order: the `client_id` of the entry, with no registration request, else dynamic client registration (`client_name` `Catervas`, `application_type` `native`, `token_endpoint_auth_method` `none`, this attempt's redirect), once per sign-in, else `sign_in_not_supported`. Catervas takes no client secret from a team file or from a user: it is a public client. No build uses a secret of Catervas's: from phase 8 Catervas Cloud adds one where a service needs it (below; changed in 0.61, ADR 0044). The redirect is `http://localhost:<port>/callback`, `localhost` because authorization servers match a registered address exactly; the listener binds `127.0.0.1` and, where there is IPv6, `[::1]`, never every address, on a free port tried up to five times, or on `callback_port` (33418 when none is given) for a given client, which fails `sign_in_failed` naming the port when it is taken. It answers the first `GET /callback` whose `state` is the attempt's, a random single-use value, with a plain page, the Catervas mark, a headline and a sentence, that says how it went from Catervas's own reason and quotes nothing the service sent, no link and no script, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; img-src data:`, `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, and closes; another path is 404 and another `state` 400, neither ending the attempt. An attempt lasts ten minutes (`sign_in_timed_out`), and a new one for the same agent and server ends the old. So does the page's Cancel: `connector.sign_in_cancel { attempt }` stops the attempt's polling or its listener, waits for that, and drops the attempt with whatever the service gave it, a grant approved already included, so a sign-in the person says yes to after Cancel is never kept; an attempt already gone or used up is not a refusal, and the answer is `{}`. `iss` (RFC 9207), when the callback carries one, must equal the metadata's `issuer`, and must be there when the metadata says `authorization_response_iss_parameter_supported`, else `sign_in_mismatch`, an error callback with another `iss` included, whose `error` is then not shown; `error=access_denied` is `access_denied`. `resource` (RFC 8707) is the protected-resource metadata's, which must be the server's address or a path above it, and is sent on the authorization, token and refresh requests. The grant, `{ issuer, resource, client_id, token_endpoint, revocation_endpoint?, access_token, refresh_token?, issued_at, expires_at?, scopes, lapsed }`, is kept in the agent's entry, in the keychain or `connectors.json` beside any keys, under the same account, and never prints. Refresh and revocation are Catervas's own form requests to the grant's endpoints, through the same check: a session setup refreshes a token that would expire within the session's wall clock and five minutes, the launch route one that would within a minute, saving a rotated refresh token before the new access token is used, under one lock per entry that connect, disconnect and the deletes of removing an agent or a server also take, so a token refreshed while the entry is removed is not kept. A refresh the service refuses with `invalid_grant`, `invalid_client` or `unauthorized_client` lapses the grant: the server is left out of sessions and `team.get` says `sign_in_again`; any other failure uses a token that is still good, and leaves the server out of that session alone when it is not. Remove deletes the entry and then asks the service, within five seconds and without failing the removal, to forget the grant (RFC 7009), the refresh token first; connecting again over another grant asks it to forget the one replaced, unless that is the grant just kept or its client's. A refreshed grant is not saved over an entry that was deleted or changed outside the daemon's lock, and the token just rotated is revoked instead. The page says "Signed in to <host>" for a signed-in row; a lapsed row says "<host> ended Catervas's sign-in. Sign in again to use it." with "Sign in again", which opens `ConnectorAdd` filled in from the team file at the sign-in; Remove says Catervas deletes the sign-in and asks the service to forget it, or, where the service has no revocation endpoint, that to remove Catervas completely the user also removes it in the service's settings. On `ConnectorAdd`, Next for a web address with no key typed asks the service first: where it offers signing in the page shows "Sign in with <issuer host>", with "for <address host>" beneath it when the two differ, opens the page in a new tab in the click itself, and checks every two seconds, then "Signed in to <host>" and on to labelling; "Use a key instead" shows the key fields, and a service that does not offer, or cannot be signed in to, shows them, with a sentence for the last two. On the command line, `catervas connect <agent> <name> --url <url> --sign-in [--client-id <id> [--callback-port <port>]] [--scope <s>]... [--tag <tool>=...]...` conflicts with `--key` and `--command`, writes `Sign in to <host> in your browser: <url>` to the error stream, opens it with the system's opener (an opener that fails is no error), waits ten minutes, writes `Signed in to <issuer>.` there, then prints the tools and where the sign-in is kept; `catervas disconnect` asks the service to forget a sign-in it held. The loopback redirect assumes the browser and the daemon share a computer, which the daemon's serving `127.0.0.1` alone guarantees (8.6).

Catervas's own apps (added in 0.58; ADR 0035, route 2; phase 7 step 03b). A service that registers no client by itself but takes a public one is signed in to with an app Catervas registered with it, so that the user makes none. `catervas_runtime::registered_apps::REGISTERED_APPS` is a static table with one `RegisteredApp` per app: its `id`, its `name` (what the screens call it), the one `host` it serves (none for an app that signs in for one of Catervas's own connectors, which serves no address), the `catervas_connector` it signs in for (if any), its `flow`, its public `client_id`, the `client_secret` a service insists on for a client it calls public (if any), the `scopes` it asks for, its `issuer`, its `token_endpoint`, a `revocation_endpoint` when it has one, an `install_url` when the app must be installed on what an agent is to read, and a `settings_url`, the page where the user removes the app at the service. It ships with no entry (changed in 0.61, ADR 0044): Catervas's GitHub App (host `api.githubcopilot.com`) and Catervas's Google app are registered in phase 8 and held by Catervas Cloud, never in a build, so no sign-in runs on the table yet, and for GitHub's address `ConnectorAdd` falls to the key fields, where a fine-grained token is pasted; the tests pass tables of their own, which the daemon takes from `DaemonState::set_registered_apps` and the command line from `CliIo`. An app is matched by the server's address alone, before any discovery, and never by what the server's metadata says, since a server could name another authorization server and receive a token from Catervas's app: the address is parsed as a URL, its scheme is `https` on port 443 (or `http` on a loopback host, which the tests need), it holds no user name or password, and its host equals the app's, ignoring ASCII case, with no trailing dot. A `client_id` of an app's in the team file is used only for the address that app serves: for any other it is refused `sign_in_failed` ("this sign-in is only for <name>'s own servers") before any request; on that address, with no `client_id` of the user's own or with the app's, the app signs in and the team file's `scopes` are ignored; any other `client_id` runs the sign-in above unchanged. GitHub's flow is the device flow (RFC 8628): Catervas posts the `client_id` with no `scope`, since a GitHub App's permissions are set on the app, to the device endpoint; the answer's `verification_uri` must be the app's own page, else `sign_in_failed` before anything is polled; the user types the `user_code` on that page and says yes; and Catervas polls the token endpoint from inside `SignIn::finish`, so that dropping the attempt, as the daemon does to an old attempt for a new one, stops the polling, first after the answer's `interval` (5 seconds when it gives none, never under 1) and then at every interval, adding 5 seconds to every later wait for each `slow_down`. `authorization_pending` waits, `access_denied` is `access_denied`, `expired_token` and ten minutes from the start are `sign_in_timed_out`, and any other `error` is `sign_in_failed`. A poll whose answer is not the service's, a connection that fails, a reply that is not a JSON object (a gateway's 502 page) or a server error with no `error` code in it, does not end the sign-in: Catervas waits the interval and polls again, and ends it `sign_in_failed` after three such answers in a row, an address refused as not `https` ending it at once. Every request to the device and token endpoints, a refresh included, sends `Accept: application/json`, since GitHub otherwise answers form-encoded, and an `error` in an answer is read before its status, since GitHub answers one with 200. The device code never leaves Catervas, and no listener, `state` or PKCE is involved. The grant is step 03's with `app` (the app's `id`; a grant kept before this has none), an empty `scopes`, the app's issuer and token endpoint, the server's address as `resource` and no revocation endpoint, since revoking needs the client secret, which is never shipped or sent; so Remove cannot ask GitHub to forget it and says where to remove Catervas. A refresh of a grant with an `app` sends no `resource`, and `bad_refresh_token` and `incorrect_client_credentials` lapse any grant, as `invalid_grant` does. GitHub keeps at most 10 tokens for a user, an app and a scope and ends the oldest, so a grant replaced by "Connect again" more than ten times can lapse an older agent's, which then shows "Sign in again". `connector.sign_in` answers `{ attempt, authorize_url, issuer, provider?, user_code?, install_url? }`, the last three only when an app signs in, `authorize_url` then being its `verification_uri`; `team.get`'s connector rows say `provider?` and `settings_url?` for a kept grant whose app the daemon's table has. On `ConnectorAdd`, where an app signs in, the page says "<Provider> lets you sign in." with "Sign in with <Provider>", no "for <host>" line, and "Catervas shows you a short code to type on <Provider>'s page."; the button shows the code in a card, "Enter this code on <Provider>:", the code large in the code face, "Only enter a code that this page shows you. Catervas never sends you a code in a chat.", "Copy the code", "Open <page>" (the page's host and path, `github.com/login/device`), which opens it in a new tab in the click itself, and "Waiting for you on <Provider>…", checking every two seconds for up to ten minutes; once the user has said yes, "Signed in to <Provider>." and, where the app has an `install_url`, "To let <Name> read private repositories, install Catervas on them on <Provider>." with the link "Install Catervas on <Provider>" in a new tab. An agent's row says "Signed in to <Provider>" in place of the address's host, a kit service's "Signed in to <Provider>." in place of its title, and Remove, where the service cannot be asked to forget the sign-in, says "To remove Catervas completely, also remove it in <Provider>'s settings." with the name a link to `settings_url` in a new tab (naming the connector for one with neither a provider nor a web address). On the command line, `catervas connect` writes `Open <verification_uri> and enter the code <user_code>.` and the same warning sentence to the error stream, opens the page, waits ten minutes, and writes `Signed in to <Provider>.`.

Signing in with Google, for Catervas's own connector (added in 0.59; ADR 0035's amendments of 2026-10-05 and 2026-10-06, ADR 0042; phase 7 step 08e). Catervas's Google app, a Desktop client in a Google Cloud project the founder owns and Catervas Cloud holds from phase 8 (changed in 0.61, ADR 0044; ADR 0048), signs an agent in for Catervas's own `google-ads` connector with the one scope `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/adwords`; the connector itself is the next paragraph's (added in 0.68), and `google-ads` is among Catervas's own connectors, so an entry that names it matches the table's entry for it. `app_for_catervas_connector(table, command, args)` answers an entry only when `command` and `args` are exactly `catervas connector <name>` for one of Catervas's own connectors and `<name>` is the entry's `catervas_connector`; a `stdio` entry with `oauth` that no entry answers is `sign_in_not_supported`, as Google's is in every build, since no build has a Google entry (changed in 0.64). The entry's flow, `AppFlow::Loopback { authorization_endpoint }`, is Catervas's own requests and not `rmcp`'s, since there is no MCP server whose metadata to discover: the listener of the sign-in above (`http://localhost:<free port>/callback`, `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]`, five tries, the first `GET /callback` with the attempt's `state`, a plain page that names the provider, ten minutes), a random `state`, a verifier of 64 characters and its S256 challenge, and an authorization address that carries exactly `client_id`, `redirect_uri`, `response_type=code`, `scope` (the entry's scopes joined by a space), `state`, `code_challenge` and `code_challenge_method=S256`, with no `resource`, since there is no MCP resource, and no `access_type`. The team file's `oauth.scopes`, when given, must each be among the entry's, else `sign_in_failed` ("Catervas's <name> sign-in asks only for <the entry's scopes, joined by a comma and a space>") before any request; none means the entry's. The callback must carry an `iss` equal to the entry's issuer, else `sign_in_mismatch`; `iss` is required here, and step 08g's live check settles whether Google sends it; `error=access_denied` is `access_denied`. The exchange posts `code`, `client_id`, `client_secret`, `redirect_uri`, `grant_type=authorization_code` and `code_verifier`, asking for JSON, and is refused `sign_in_failed` when `token_type` is not `Bearer` (ASCII case ignored: "<name> did not give Catervas a bearer token"), when there is no `refresh_token` ("<name> did not give Catervas a lasting sign-in") and when the granted `scope` lacks one that was asked for ("you did not allow Catervas all it asks of <name>; sign in again and tick every box"). The grant is step 03's with `app` the entry's `id`, the entry's issuer and token endpoint, the issuer as `resource` (bookkeeping, which nothing reads for an app's grant), no revocation endpoint and the granted scopes. A refresh of a grant of an app that has a `client_secret` sends it, looked up by `app` in the daemon's table (the command line's is `REGISTERED_APPS`) on each refresh and never kept in the grant, and sends no `resource`; a grant whose `app` the table lacks, as in every build before Catervas Cloud (phase 8), has lapsed at once, with no request. Google's Testing status ends a refresh token after 7 days, which `invalid_grant` makes "Sign in again", and its consent screen warns that the app is unverified until Google has verified the scope, which waits for Catervas's website (phase 8; ADR 0042's amendment, ADR 0048). Remove deletes the local grant and cannot revoke it, since Google's revocation ends every agent's grant of the project at once; it says where to remove Catervas, `https://myaccount.google.com/connections`. The daemon and `catervas connect` sign a Catervas connector in as any server that signs in: `connector.sign_in` answers `provider` and no `user_code`; an attempt is bound to the agent, the server's name and the server's whole transport, so one made for the pair serves neither an `http` server of the same name nor the pair with other settings (`sign_in_unknown`); and `connector.sign_in_status`'s words name the provider where an app signs in. The launch route answers a signed-in Catervas connector with its command and an empty environment, never a token, after refreshing a grant that expires within a minute, and 403 `sign_in_again` for a lapsed one; a session's setup refreshes it as it does an `http` one's and leaves a lapsed one out; its tools are listed with no token, since a `stdio` server takes no bearer; and `team.get` says `auth: oauth`, `provider` and `revokes: false`. On the command line, `catervas connect <agent> <name>` writes `Sign in to <provider> in your browser: <address>` and then `Signed in to <provider>.` to the error stream.

Google Ads, Catervas's own connector (added in 0.68; ADR 0038, ADR 0042, ADR 0044; phase 7 step 08f). `google-ads` is one of `CATERVAS_CONNECTORS` (`osv`, `google-ads`, and since 0.75 `fx`), and the Marketing Specialist's kit lists it after Kit as `catervas connector google-ads` with `oauth` for the one scope `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/adwords`: `list_accounts`, `report` and `keyword_ideas` are `network` ("list ad accounts", "read ad results", "find search words"), and its seven writes, `create_search_campaign`, `add_ad_group`, `add_keywords`, `add_negative_keywords`, `add_responsive_search_ad`, `set_campaign_budget` and `set_campaign_status`, are `external_effect` and `plan_approved` (5.6), none with an allowance. The server is a shim: `catervas connector google-ads` lists the ten tools itself, so its list is pinned offline, and forwards each call to the daemon, which holds the grant, checks the plan against the log, calls Google and records what it created. It reads `CATERVAS_CONNECTOR_URL` and `CATERVAS_CONNECTOR_TICKET` from its environment, which the launcher (`catervas connector run`) sets beside `KEPT_ENV` and the entry's keys, and nothing else: the ticket from the launch route's answer and the address `http://127.0.0.1:<the port daemon.json names>/connector/call`. With neither set it still lists its tools and a call is the tool error "Google Ads runs only inside a Catervas session". At each call it refuses an address that is not exactly that form, follows no redirect, uses no proxy, waits at most 120 seconds, reads at most 4 MiB, sends `{ tool, arguments }` with `Authorization: Bearer <ticket>`, and answers the daemon's `{ ok }` as the tool's text and its `{ error }` as a tool error. The launch route answers a Catervas connector that signs in `{ command, args, env: {}, cwd, ticket }`: the ticket is 32 random bytes in hex, its sha256 kept on the session by server until the session ends (`end_session`), never a token. `POST /connector/call` sits outside the daemon-token layer, behind `require_project`; it compares the ticket's sha256 with every live session's in constant time (401 otherwise), refuses a stopped session (`session_stopped`), and runs the call as that session's agent. The route's checks, in order: the session's connector `google-ads` and a ticket made for it (`connector_not_in_session`); the agent's entry being the kit's, `matches_kit` (`google_ads_not_kit`, so a custom entry naming the same command reaches nothing, whatever its tags); the tool tagged by the session, not `denied`, and for a write `external_effect` and plan-marked (`tool_not_tagged`, `tool_denied`, `tool_not_plan_marked`); for a write, an active plan (`no_active_marketing_plan`); the agent's grant, refreshed when it will not last 120 seconds, waiting at most 10 (`sign_in_again`, `connector_not_confirmed`, `sign_in_failed`); the input (`google_ads_input`); for a write, what the plan covers (`not_in_marketing_plan: <why>`); then Google (`google_ads_refused`, `google_ads_failed`). A write holds one daemon-wide lock from reading the plan and the campaigns made through Google's answer and the record of `marketing_campaign.created`; reads take none. Just before it sends anything to Google it reads the plan again, and sends nothing (`no_active_marketing_plan`) when the active plan is no longer the one the write was checked against; and whatever ends or replaces a plan (the owner's end, an approval that replaces one, the dates' rule, and the pause on a plan's end, 6.5) takes the same lock first, so a write in flight finishes before the end is recorded (amended in 0.68, from step 08f's landing review); so does a removal of an agent's Google Ads sign-in (6.5), from its pause through its write of the team, so that no enable runs between them (added in 0.72 after review). The writes' lock is always taken before the team's, never after it. Calls to Google never go through `call_as` (ADR 0042's amendment): the daemon is the server. Google's API is v25 over REST at the fixed `https://googleads.googleapis.com/v25` (a Catervas release moves it), never an argument or an input, with the grant as the bearer and no developer token (Google ended them on 2026-09-09) and no `login-customer-id`, so only accounts the sign-in reaches directly; it follows no redirect, uses no proxy, waits 25 seconds, reads at most 4 MiB in chunks, and says Google's refusal in Catervas's words with Google's `message` cut at 300 characters and quoted, a `PERMISSION_DENIED` on keyword ideas being "Google has not yet allowed Catervas's app to give keyword ideas." (Explorer access blocks that service; the access level is Catervas Cloud's project's, ADR 0044). GAQL is built only from fixed text and checked values (an account is `123-456-7890`, a resource name `customers/<ten digits>/(campaigns|adGroups)/<id>`, a day `YYYY-MM-DD`), never a free query, and a search sends `{ query }` alone. The reads: `list_accounts` (at most 20 accounts, each `{ account, name, currency, time_zone, manager }`), `report { account, kind, from, to }` (`campaigns`, `ad_groups`, `keywords`, `search_terms` or `ads`, the days at most 366 apart, at most 500 rows, `more` when cut, the cost a decimal of the account's currency) and `keyword_ideas { account, words, language, locations }` (1 to 10 words, Google's numeric constant ids, at most 100 ideas of `{ text, avg_monthly_searches, competition, low_bid, high_bid }`). The writes: `create_search_campaign { account, plan_campaign, name, bidding, max_cpc?, locations, languages }` makes, in one atomic `googleAds:mutate`, a budget and a campaign named `<plan id> <key>: <name>`, a `SEARCH` campaign created `PAUSED` on Google Search alone with its dates (`start_date_time` the later of the plan campaign's first day and the UTC date two days after today at `00:00:00`, so no time zone, east of UTC included, makes it the past, `end_date_time` its last day at `23:59:59`, in the account's time zone), `DOES_NOT_CONTAIN_EU_POLITICAL_ADVERTISING`, `maximize_clicks` (Google's `target_spend`, `max_cpc` its ceiling) or `maximize_conversions` (no `max_cpc`), and a criterion for each of 1 to 20 locations and 1 to 10 languages; `add_ad_group`, `add_keywords` and `add_negative_keywords` (1 to 50 words of 1 to 80 characters, `exact`, `phrase` or `broad`) and `add_responsive_search_ad` (3 to 15 headlines of at most 30 characters, 2 to 4 descriptions of at most 90, an `https` address with a host and no user information, paths of at most 15) create what they name `ENABLED`, only the campaign being created paused; `set_campaign_budget` and `set_campaign_status` (`paused` or `enabled`) change one campaign. The budget Google keeps (`campaign_budget`, `catervas-core`): over the campaign's run, from its first day to its last, both included, a run of 3 to 90 days takes a total budget (`CUSTOM_PERIOD`, `total_amount_micros`), the plan campaign's budget, which Google never bills past (a key has one campaign across a plan's whole lineage, so nothing of its budget was spent before the campaign was made), and any other run a daily budget of what is left divided by the days, rounded down to the hundredth, which bounds Google's own charging while Catervas is not running; every budget is `STANDARD` and not shared; a currency with no minor unit (`ZERO_DECIMAL`) takes whole units, rounded down; a create, a change of budget and enabling a campaign each read the ad account's currency first and are refused when it is not the plan's (a plan in another currency cannot replace one, `marketing_plan_currency` in 6.5's proposal, and the check holds beside it). What the active plan covers (`check_ads_write`, pure, refusing in a sentence after `not_in_marketing_plan: `), with the plan's lineage being the active plan and every plan it replaces through the whole chain: every write is in the plan's `google_ads_account`; a create is for a key the active plan has, with no campaign recorded for that key under any plan of the lineage, and its last day is not before its first possible day; a write under a campaign (an ad group's campaign is read from Google), a new budget or enabling needs the campaign recorded for a key the active plan has under a plan of the lineage; a total budget's new amount is from what the campaign spent to the plan campaign's budget and a daily one at most what is left divided by the days left; enabling needs today within the plan campaign's dates, its spend below its budget, the plan's Google Ads spend below the plan's, and what Google holds for the campaign no more than the active plan gives its key, since a plan that replaces another and lowers a key's budget or shortens its dates leaves Google's total budget and end as the first plan made them: a total budget at most the plan campaign's budget, a daily one at most what is left of it over the days Google still runs the campaign (from its own first day, or today, to its own last), and an end no later than the plan campaign's, which Catervas cannot change; a figure Google did not give refuses it (amended in 0.68, from step 08f's landing review); pausing covers any campaign recorded under a plan of the lineage, the active plan having dropped its key or not, and with no active plan the hook refuses it first (`no_active_marketing_plan`). Spend is read from Google for the call, one `Search` for each ad account the lineage's campaigns are in (changed in 0.72, so that enabling and the cap count one spend) of those campaigns' `cost_micros`, with each campaign's budget amounts and its start and end, from the day before the first was made to tomorrow, rounded up from micros to hundredths; a spend read that fails refuses a budget change and enabling, and pausing needs none.
