The hard stop's reads and pauses (6.5, added in 0.72) are not a session's calls: the watch and the removals of a sign-in (`connector.disconnect`, a save, a start, a template and a retirement, 6.5) take a Marketing Specialist's grant in the daemon (the entry that `matches_kit`, its stored sign-in, no ticket) and send only a fixed status query and a `PAUSED` mutation for each campaign, under the same lock. Nothing here deletes, or touches billing, who may use the account, conversion tracking, another campaign type or a campaign Catervas did not make for the plan. The kit row before the launch: until Catervas Cloud signs customers in to Google (phase 8, ADR 0044, ADR 0048), `team.get`'s kit row carries `at_launch: true` for an entry whose `oauth` is on Catervas's own connector when the daemon's table has no app for it, and the agent's page shows the service's About and "{service} comes with Catervas’s web launch." in place of Connect. The founder's live check is phase 8's.

Catervas Cloud (added in 0.61; ADR 0044; it replaces 0.60's paragraph on the user's own sign-in apps, which were dropped with phase 7 step 03f before they were built). Catervas is open source, so no build carries a credential of Catervas's, before or after the launch (8.6). Until Catervas Cloud runs (phase 8), nothing that needs an app of Catervas's is offered: no build has an entry in the table above, GitHub's server (`https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/`) is connected with a pasted fine-grained token, sent as `Authorization: Bearer`, and Catervas's own `google-ads` connector cannot be signed in to (`sign_in_not_supported`). The code read `CATERVAS_GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` at build time until phase 7 step 08e's Task 8 removed it (changed in 0.64): `REGISTERED_APPS` is empty in every build, a test fails for any source that reads a variable at build time with `env!` or `option_env!`, bar the names Cargo itself sets for a build (`CARGO_PKG_*`, `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*`, `CARGO_CRATE_NAME` and `CARGO_BIN_NAME`), and the loopback sign-in with PKCE, the refresh and the table's matching stay, for the sign-in through Catervas Cloud to build on. From phase 8, before the web launch (ADR 0048), Catervas Cloud, a service Catervas runs, signs users in; the service and its hosting are planned separately in `catervas-ops`, Catervas's private operations repository (changed in 0.73, ADR 0047). Its free tier holds Catervas's GitHub App and Catervas's Google app and their secrets, server side. The local Catervas starts each sign-in through it and refreshes each grant through it, with the user's Catervas account; Catervas Cloud answers the authorization address, adds the secret on the exchange and each refresh, and hands the tokens straight back, storing none, and they are kept with the agent's keys, as every grant is. A Catervas account is signed in to once per computer, in the browser; its session is the user's own credential, kept in the OS keychain, and Catervas Cloud's fair-use limits are per account. Phase 8 plans how the account signs in, GitHub's flow, which the founder wants to be a single click, and the screens.

GitHub with a pasted key (added in 0.65; ADR 0044; phase 7 step 07c). Until Catervas Cloud signs users in (above), the Product Manager's and the Architect's kits each carry GitHub's official server, `https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/`, as an `http` connector named `github` with `credential_keys: [GITHUB_KEY]`, `Authorization: Bearer {GITHUB_KEY}`, a `key_page` and no `oauth`, so `team.get`'s row says `auth: keys` and the connect page shows its key field. The kit format takes a sign-in or keys, never both (`oauth_with_keys`, `oauth_header_conflict`), and a sign-in would be `sign_in_not_supported`, since GitHub registers no client by itself. The key is a fine-grained personal access token: with one, GitHub's server lists every tool and its API enforces the key's permissions (GitHub's changelog of 2026-01-28), so the listing is the same for every user's key and a pin can hold; a classic key reaches every repository the user can and the server hides tools by its scopes, which differ by user, so the kits do not take one. The entries narrow the server with headers, which the listing and every session send as they send the key: the Product Manager's `X-MCP-Toolsets: issues,projects`; the Architect's `X-MCP-Toolsets: repos,pull_requests` and `X-MCP-Readonly: true`, which keeps only the read tools. Neither entry names `X-MCP-Tools`, since GitHub makes the server fail to start on a tool name it does not know, so one tool GitHub removed would stop every user's GitHub until a Catervas release, whereas a toolset that loses a tool only drops it, which the live pin test reports; nor `X-MCP-Exclude-Tools`, since a `denied` tool is never offered already, the pin comparison runs both ways and GitHub's page is silent on an unknown name there; nor `X-MCP-Lockdown`, which hides the public issue details of users without push access, the reports a Product Manager imports, and which GitHub calls a best-effort content filter, not a security boundary. Three fences hold what an agent may do: GitHub refuses what the key may not do; the headers keep other toolsets off the listing; and the tags decide what is offered, so a `denied` tool and a tool the kit does not name (`tool_not_tagged`) are never offered, and nothing that deletes, administers, touches secrets or workflows, merges, pushes or changes a file is. A tool GitHub lists that the kit does not name is never offered until a pin update tags it, and the update's rule is `denied`, with no label. The Product Manager's tags: eight reads are `network` (`issue_read`, `list_issues`, `search_issues`, `list_issue_types`, `list_issue_fields`, `get_label`, `projects_list` and `projects_get`); `issue_write` and `add_issue_comment` are `external_effect` with no allowance, so each call waits for the human, who sees its whole input, since an issue or a comment is published to everyone who can see the repository; `sub_issue_write`, `update_issue_comment` and `projects_write` are `denied`. A kit tags a tool, not an argument, and both writes reach pull requests too, a pull request being an issue to GitHub's API (`issue_write`'s `update` method edits, closes and reassigns), so the skill `using-product-sources` forbids giving either a pull request's number, and whether a key with Issues write alone can close one is recorded by the live check, not fixed. The Architect's tags: fifteen reads are `network` (`search_code`, `get_file_contents`, `list_branches`, `list_commits`, `get_commit`, `search_commits`, `list_tags`, `get_tag`, `list_releases`, `get_latest_release`, `get_release_by_tag`, `search_repositories`, `list_pull_requests`, `pull_request_read` and `search_pull_requests`) and `list_repository_collaborators`, a list of who may change a repository, is `denied`; GitHub refuses `pull_request_read`'s `get_status` and `get_check_runs` to its key, which `using-architecture-sources` says. The two entries share the address, the key's name and `about`, and differ in headers, `key_page`, tools, labels, `why` and `setup`, so they hash differently, and a team file that gives an Architect the Product Manager's wider entry fails `matches_kit`. "Get your key" opens `key_page`, GitHub's template address for a fine-grained key (`name`, `description`, `expires_in`, and `<permission>=<level>`, which GitHub's form validates), with a life of 366 days, GitHub's longest dated one: the Product Manager's asks for repository Issues read and write, the Architect's for Contents and Pull requests read. Neither sets the resource owner, which is the user's to choose, nor organisation Projects, which GitHub's form accepts only when the owner is an organisation, and most users' owner is their account, so the Product Manager's setup says to add it for an organisation's boards. A fine-grained key has one owner, the user's account or one organisation, so a user whose code sits under both connects GitHub for one of them per agent until the sign-in; an organisation that requires approval leaves the key `pending`, reading public resources only, until an administrator approves it; it does not reach Projects owned by a user account, so the Product Manager reads an organisation's boards only; an organisation may cap a key's life below a year, or need the user signed in to its single sign-on when the key is made, which both setups say; and every key reads all public repositories. GitHub's documentation (read 2026-10-06) says its server is available to every GitHub user whatever the plan and lists Copilot as a prerequisite only for Copilot's own editors, which Catervas is not, so the setups say nothing about Copilot; a key GitHub refuses shows the connect page's words for a refused key, and the founder's live check (step 07c, Task 6) settles whether an account that never turned Copilot on connects. Remove deletes the key from the keychain, as for every keyed service, and the key works at GitHub until it ends or the user deletes it there. The live pin test reads `CATERVAS_KIT_GITHUB_GITHUB_KEY` once for both kits, since they share a connector name. From phase 8 (ADR 0048) each entry signs in through Catervas Cloud instead: it loses `headers.Authorization`, `credential_keys` and `key_page`, gains `oauth: {}` (the table's GitHub entry, matched by host) and keeps its `X-MCP-*` headers; its hash changes, so every agent connected with a key sees "Connect again"; and Catervas's GitHub App then asks for repository Contents and Pull requests read, Issues read and write, and organisation Projects read (ADR 0044's amendment of 2026-10-06), or the Product Manager's two writes stop at the switch.

A user's own skills (added in 0.40; ADR 0034). Before any kit, the user can give one agent, or the whole team, a skill in the Agent Skills format: a folder with a `SKILL.md` and the files it refers to. The team's lives in `.catervas/skills/<name>/` and one agent's in `.catervas/agents/<agent_id>/skills/<name>/`, both read from the project's root, never a task's worktree, as `team.yaml` is; agents cannot write `.catervas/` (5.3). A name resolves agent, then team, then role. An agent's pin replaces the team's skill of that name whatever its state, so a skill waiting for review never falls back silently to the team's. A team or agent skill whose name is one of the shipped skills replaces it only when the person said so: `skill_save` and `skill_confirm` with such a name are refused `skill_name_taken` unless they carry `replace_shipped: true`. While such a replacement is `in_use`, the sessions that load skills (8.2) leave the shipped skill out of the prompt and load the replacement on demand; the sessions given one Catervas tool alone have no `Skill` tool, so they keep the shipped skill in their prompt.

A skill is instructions an agent follows, so the person reads all of it before it is used: adding or editing one shows the whole `SKILL.md` and every file first, and the add is the confirmation. `team.yaml` pins each skill, `skills: [{ name, sha256 }]` at the top level for the team and on each agent for that agent, at most 20 in a list and a name once in it (`skill_name_twice`). `sha256` is `skill_sha256`: the sha256, in lower-case hex, of the canonical JSON (compact, keys sorted at every depth) of `{ <relative path>: <sha256 of the file's bytes> }` over every file in the folder. As a connector is (ADR 0030), a skill is confirmed on this computer, because `.catervas/` travels with the repository and a clone, a pull or a hand edit could change a skill's instructions and its pin together. The states, checked in this order as `skills.list` computes them for one agent: `replaced`, a team row when the agent pins a skill of that name and a role row when a team or agent skill of that name is `in_use`; `missing`, a pinned folder that is not there; `review`, the folder exists but its hash differs from the pin, or the newest `skill.added`, `skill.changed` or `skill.confirmed` for it in this machine's log (8.4, never committed) does not carry the pin's hash, or a `skill.removed` follows it, or there is none, or reading the folder or the checks below refuse it; and `in_use` otherwise, the only state a skill loads in. `skill_confirm`, with the hash the person saw, reads the folder, refuses `skill_hash_mismatch` unless its hash is that one, refuses whatever the checks refuse with that refusal's code, writes the hash as the pin when it differs, then records `skill.confirmed`, so a skill whose folder or pin changed outside Catervas is used again only after the person has read the folder as it is now. Shipped skills are Catervas's own, neither pinned nor confirmed. In the browser's key mapper the paths of a command's or answer's `files` are names, not wire keys, and cross unchanged (added in 0.42).

The checks, which hold in `skill_save`, `skill_confirm` and when a session assembles its skills. `SKILL.md` opens with a `---` line and closes its frontmatter with another (CRLF is read as LF); the frontmatter is a YAML mapping read under ADR 0007's options. `name` and `description` are strings: a missing `SKILL.md`, no frontmatter, a frontmatter that is not a mapping or a missing `name` is `skill_frontmatter_invalid`; `name` is lower-case letters and digits joined by single hyphens, at most 64 characters, and its folder's (`skill_name_invalid`, `skill_name_mismatch`); `description` has 1 to 1,024 characters, counted in Unicode scalar values (`skill_description_invalid`). Limits: `SKILL.md` at most 32 KiB; at most 16 files, `SKILL.md` among them, each at most 64 KiB, UTF-8 text with no NUL; 256 KiB in all; a path of at most 3 parts, the file's name included, each part matching `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,99}$`, so none starts with a dot, and no link anywhere (`skill_too_large`, `skill_too_many_files`, `skill_file_not_text`, `skill_path_invalid`). A skill may not run commands or attach files, because Claude Code does both when a skill loads, and an attachment is read by its own code, which no hook sees: a `SKILL.md` holding `` !` `` or ```` ```! ```` anywhere, or a line whose first characters after spaces are three or more backticks or tildes followed by `!`, is refused `skill_runs_commands`; one with an `@` is refused `skill_attaches_files` unless the character before it is an ASCII letter or digit or one of `._%+-`, as in an email address (Claude Code attaches `@<path>` after any whitespace, a byte order mark included, and after 。、？！). A skill names a bundled file as a path or a Markdown link, which the agent reads with `Read`, which the hook judges. Every other frontmatter field (`allowed-tools`, `hooks`, `model`, `context` and the rest) is accepted, dropped from the session's copy, and listed to the person ("Catervas ignores: allowed-tools, hooks").

The commands (sent through `here_or_sent` from the command line and as `command` through the RPC from the browser) are `skill_save { level, agent?, files, replace_shipped? }`, which adds a skill, or replaces the one of its name, `files` mapping each relative path to its text; `skill_remove { level, agent?, name }`; and `skill_confirm { level, agent?, name, sha256, replace_shipped? }`; `level` is `team`, or `agent` with the agent's id. A save writes the new folder beside its place as `.<name>.new-<random>`, moves any old folder to `.<name>.old-<random>`, renames the new one into place (`rename(2)` will not replace a folder that is not empty), deletes the old one, then writes the pin in the team file, then records `skill.added`, or `skill.changed` for a name already pinned; the pin is written before the event for a confirmation as well. A removal deletes the folder, unpins it and records `skill.removed`, and for a `missing` skill it unpins and records and does not fail. The refusals are `skill_unknown`, a name not pinned there, `skill_hash_mismatch`, `skill_name_taken`, `skill_limit_reached`, the twenty-first of a list, `agent_unknown`, and each check's code; a refusal writes nothing. A whole-team write keeps the pins: `team.save` and `agent.replace` keep the top-level `skills` and each kept agent's `skills` as the team file holds them, whatever the browser sends (an agent a save adds has none), and `template.apply` keeps the top-level `skills` and the skills of each agent it keeps, an agent it removes losing its pins as removing an agent does; only the three commands change a pin. Removing an agent leaves its skill folders, which are the user's files, unpinned and unused. A saved team template carries no skills. `catervas skill list [--agent <id>]`, `catervas skill show <name> (--team | --agent <id>)`, `catervas skill add <folder> (--team | --agent <id>) [--yes] [--replace]`, `catervas skill remove` and `catervas skill confirm <name> (--team | --agent <id>) <hash> [--replace]` are F9's.

What stays: a confirmed skill can steer its agent within the agent's own tiers and connectors in every later session, and its description is listed in each; the reading before adding is the defence against the instructions themselves (8.6). A skill that runs scripts is not supported.

The kit file (added in 0.44; ADR 0036). `roles/<role>/kit.yaml` holds `role` (the folder's), `skills` (names, at most 20) and `connectors` (at most 20), validated by `docs/schemas/kit.schema.json` and then by the loader in `catervas-roles`, which embeds the file with its skills and refuses, naming the field as a JSON pointer and a code, a `role` that is not the folder's (`kit_role_mismatch`); a skill with no `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` (`kit_skill_missing`), one `role.yaml` names too (`kit_skill_in_role`; it stays in the prompt there), or one the checks of a user's skill refuse, with that refusal's code (6.7, ADR 0034); a connector name twice (`kit_connector_twice`). A connector is `stdio`, `http` or `container`. A `stdio` or `http` one takes the team file's fields (`command`, `args`, `url`, `headers`, `credential_keys`, `oauth`), each refusal `validate_team` gives the same entry (so a kit cannot ship a key in a header or an argument), and the setup copy: `title`, `about`, `why`, `setup`, `key_page` (an `https` address, required when it takes keys: `key_page_missing`) and `labels`, a tool to what it does in the user's words (`label_not_a_tool` for one that is not a tool); a field a transport does not take or lacks is `kit_field_not_allowed` or `kit_field_missing`. Every connector has `tools`, each `network`, `external_effect` or `denied`, never `read`, and a tool with no value is a schema error. A `stdio` connector starts only `npx`, `bunx`, `uvx` or `pipx run` (also by path or as `.cmd`/`.exe`), with its first non-flag argument the package at an exact version, `name@1.2.3` or `name==1.2.3`, and none of `-p`, `--package`, `--from`, `--spec`, `--with`, `--registry` or `--index-url`; or an absolute path to a binary Catervas ships (`catervas`, `catervas-…`). Any other command, every shell and every other runner (`sh`, `pnpm`, `npm`, `yarn`, `bun`, `uv`, `deno`, `docker`, `node`, `python`) is `package_not_pinned` (at `command` or `args`), so the code Catervas runs changes only with a Catervas release and its pin review. A `container` connector, a server Catervas runs in Docker itself with an `image`, `module_root` and `args` and no copy, is only the UI/UX Designer's built-in browser (`container_not_builtin`) with its image pinned by `@sha256:` (`image_not_pinned`). `allowances` map a tool tagged `external_effect` to `{ calls, what }` (`allowance_not_external`); phase 7 step 05b reads them. Catervas's own words never say "MCP", "OAuth" or "token", so no field of the copy holds one in any case (`copy_word_refused`), with one exception the founder made on 2026-10-02: `setup` may quote a service's own label so the user finds it on the service's page ("On Slack's page, copy the value labelled ‘Bot User OAuth Token’."). A quoted label is the text after `‘` up to `’`, after `“` up to `”`, or after `"` up to `"`, on one line, at most 60 characters; the ASCII apostrophe never opens one, an opening mark with no closing one quotes nothing, and a longer span is checked as the rest is. Every role ships a `kit.yaml`; none has a service before step 06, and only the Designer's has the browser.

Kit skills and kit connectors (added in 0.44; ADR 0036). A kit's skills live in the role's `skills/` folder, are Catervas's own (neither pinned nor confirmed) and load on demand after the agent's and the team's: one name resolves agent, team, kit, a pin of the name at either level standing for the kit's skill whatever its state. They count among the shipped names (a team or agent skill of that name needs `replace_shipped`), `skills.list` lists one as a `role` row after the role's rows, `replaced` while the agent's or the team's skill of that name is in use, and `skill.get`'s role level answers its `SKILL.md` as the session's copy holds it. A kit's `stdio` or `http` connector is connected by name: `catervas connect <agent> <name>` with neither `--command` nor `--url`, or the browser's `{ name, source: "kit" }`. The entry is written whole into the team file, `{ name, source: kit, transport, ... }`, with every field from the kit and the kit's tags as its `tools`, held to every rule of a custom entry and hashed with `"source": "kit"` added so that every kept hash stands and a relabelled entry needs "Connect again". `connector_connect` refuses a `source: kit` entry that is not exactly the one its agent's role's kit gives (`connector_not_in_kit`), which also refuses a service of another role's kit. Connecting lists the service's tools to prove the key or the sign-in works, and writes the kit's tags: a tool the service lists and the kit does not tag is `tool_not_tagged`, and a pinned tool the service dropped costs nothing. A request for a kit service carries `tags: {}` (`kit_names_these` otherwise), and the command line refuses `--arg`, `--header`, `--key`, `--tag`, `--client-id`, `--callback-port`, `--scope` and `--sign-in` with it, reads a value for each of the kit's keys from standard input after "Make a key at <key_page>, then paste <KEY>:" on the error stream, and signs in when the kit's service does. A kit entry that is not what its role's kit says now (a release tagged a tool `denied`, or changed a field) is left out of the session's `mcp.json` and registration and shown `connect_again`; one whose service the kit no longer has is shown `not_in_kit`, with "Remove" alone. A team file or a clone that forges a `source: kit` entry with wider tags hashes differently from anything kept and is refused at connect and left out at session setup; the launch route and the hook take only what a session's registration holds and re-check the kept hash. Every shipped `stdio` and `http` kit connector is pinned: `crates/runtime/tests/live_kit_pins.rs`, run with `CATERVAS_LIVE_TESTS=1` and each key from `CATERVAS_KIT_<NAME>_<KEY>` (a bearer from `CATERVAS_KIT_<NAME>_BEARER` for one that signs in), fails naming the connector and the unset variable, and on any drift with "a pin update re-reviews every tag"; `pin_drift` names the tools a service added and dropped.

**Service logos and info buttons (added in 0.79; standalone plan `agent-page-logos`).** On the agent page every connected service shows its logo beside its name: a mark bundled with the web app under `apps/web/src/assets/services/`, named for the kit connector (`notion.svg`, `google-ads.svg`), never fetched from the service, so no company learns the user's address and the page works offline. Playwright has its own mark; a connector with no mark, a user-added one included, shows a plug. The logo is decorative: the service's name stands beside it. A kit service's row reads logo, name, its `about` and an info button (ⓘ). `about` is the row's visible line, at most six words; `why` sits behind the button, at most twenty, and a test holds both (a kit from elsewhere is not held to it). Every other note on the agent page (what each permission means, what the effort levels cost, the custom connector's warnings, pause, replace and retire, skills and each skill's description (changed in 0.80), and the Procurement Specialist's orders, sites and mailbox) sits behind an info button too, which opens on hover, focus or tap and closes on Escape; headings, labels, statuses that ask for action, counts and buttons stay visible. Rewording this copy asks nobody to connect again, because it sits outside the entry the connector is hashed by.

### 6.8 UI/UX Designer (added in 0.31)

Mandate: a developer focused on the interface (ADR 0026; `docs/design/designer-chats-templates.md`). It explores the app, plans a change to it, and implements the change once the Product Manager approves the plan. With the Software Developer it is the only role that changes code, beside the DevOps Engineer's incident fixes (6.9; changed in 0.32): its tasks are not held to the team's `document_paths` (5.3), and they work on `feature/CTV-<n>` or `fix/CTV-<n>` by the contract's `change` (5.14). The team builder suggests one (4.1), named Iris, "Makes it clear, calm and easy to use", with the `extra-1` picture and the tag "UX" in its own colour, pale clay; the user may untick it (the founder's choices of 2026-09-30).

Produces: design plans, diffs, commits on task branches, completion notes.

Cannot: change code before the Product Manager approves the task's plan; modify contracts; accept work; push to shared branches without the `git_remote` grant; touch files outside the contract's allowed paths.

Default tools: read, write_workspace, execute, git_local, the Developer's, and the team's two permission answers apply to it as to the Developer (5.6). Default model: the Developer's, Claude Opus 5.5 at `high`. It ships six skills: `ux-review-heuristics`, `wcag-accessibility-checks`, `brand-and-design-tokens`, `plain-language-interface-wording`, `writing-mockups`, and `responsive-and-phone-checks`. Its `brand-and-design-tokens` reads `docs/marketing/brand/brand-kit.md` first when it exists and takes the colours and the voice from it, then finds where the project keeps its design tokens (added in 0.55; the kit is the Marketing Specialist's, 6.5).

Its flow. Every task assigned to a Designer runs this way, whatever its risk. The task's latest `design_plan.*` event, read from the log at every tick, decides which session runs next:

1. Explore. With no plan yet, or with the last one returned, Catervas starts the Designer's `explore` session (8.2) in the task's worktree. It holds the read tier's built-ins whatever the agent's tiers, its every call is held to the read tier by the hook and Catervas's tool server, and a call to its browser to `network` (changed in 0.33), it runs no commands, and it has six Catervas tools: `catervas_read_task`, `catervas_read_board`, `catervas_read_rules`, `catervas_read_criteria`, `catervas_read_decisions`, and `catervas_propose_design_plan`, and a seventh, `catervas_check_page`, with the browser (added in 0.33). With its Playwright connector on and the app's preview set (5.6), it reads the project's files and also opens the app in the browser, and its message says so; browsing before the plan is approved is intended, since the plan gate holds only writes, commands, git and, from 0.39, a connector's `external_effect` call (changed in 0.33, from no browser). When the last plan was returned, the message holds the Product Manager's reason as untrusted text (8.6). The session ends with `catervas_propose_design_plan { plan }`, which records `design_plan.proposed`. The plan is plain text of 200 to 8,000 characters. It opens with a summary for the user of 20 to 600 characters and a blank line, as a note does (5.4), and says what the agent saw, what it will change, and what it will leave alone. The tool is refused with `design_plan_refused` outside the Designer's `explore` session of its task, and for a plan outside those bounds.
2. The Product Manager's approval. With a plan proposed, Catervas starts the active Product Manager's `verify` session about the task. The plan is in its message, and its one Catervas tool is `catervas_decide_design_plan { approve, reason }`, which records `design_plan.approved` or `design_plan.returned`. The tool is refused with `design_plan_refused` from any other agent or session, for a blank reason, and when no plan waits for a decision. While the team has no active Product Manager, the task waits. The human's own gates are unchanged (5.4, 5.16), and the human is not asked to approve the plan.
3. Implement. With the plan approved, Catervas starts the Designer's `implement` session with the agent's full tiers and the approved plan in its message. From there the task is an ordinary one: a completion note, `verifying`, the review (5.4), and acceptance.

A session that ends without its one answer, an explore session with no plan or a decision session with no decision, is started again, and each restart counts toward the contract's sessions allowance (5.5). Each returned plan counts against the task's iteration limit, and when the returns reach it the governor escalates the task with `iterations` (5.2). The order is the governor's to enforce, not the prompt's. In any session of a Designer's task whose latest plan is not approved, the hook and Catervas's tool server refuse the Designer's `write_workspace`, `execute`, `git_local` and `git_remote` calls with `design_plan_not_approved`, and the hook refuses its `external_effect` connector calls the same way, before any is asked about (5.6; added in 0.39). They read the plan from the log at each call, so a plan proposed during a session closes the gate at once. A Designer's session that is about no task is held as not approved.

Its reviewer is the Architect, else a Developer (5.1, 5.4).

What the web app shows. Setup and the Team page draw each agent's picture in a ring of its role's colour. On the Team page, when the team has a Designer and a Developer, the cost section says that the Designer uses the Developer's model, so the Designer adds to what a day costs, and that planning costs a little too, and it links to Costs. The board says "Planning" for a Designer in its `explore` session, and `team.activity` says "Planning" and the task's title for it too. While a plan the Designer proposed waits for a decision on a task in progress, the Designer's activity line is "Waiting for" the active Product Manager's name "to approve a plan", about that task. Each card on the Team page says its agent's activity line in place of "On the team", and "Paused" for a paused agent. A Designer's task page opens its plan tab with the latest plan, signed with its day and time and read from `task.get`'s `design_plan` (`plan`, `state` `proposed`, `approved` or `returned`, and the `reason` once decided; null when there is none). A card above it says one of three things: the plan waits for the active Product Manager, and the user need do nothing; it was approved, so the Designer is changing the page as the plan says, with the time and the reason; or it was sent back, so the Designer is looking again and will write a new plan, with the time, the reason, and how many plans have been sent back of the number that stops the task ("If a third is sent back"). A decision counts only for the plan it followed. Times say "today" for the day the page is read, in UTC. While the task is in progress, the page's heading says the same, and its lead counts the plans ("First plan.") in place of the tries. The task's history words the three `design_plan.*` events.

Its browser and its design review (added in 0.33; ADR 0026). The Designer opens the app in the preview Catervas runs in Docker's sandbox (8.3), through the Playwright connector (5.6), in its `explore` and `implement` sessions and in its design review of a Software Developer's change to the interface (5.4). It cannot be assigned a task while it cannot open the app: the governor refuses the assignment with `preview_not_set` when the team has no preview and with `designer_needs_sandbox` when the sandbox is off or Docker cannot be reached (checked once a minute at most), and with `designer_needs_browser` when its Playwright connector is off, so that Catervas gives it no work, as the agent page says. A process that has not said what runs previews, a command handled in its own process among them, runs none, so the assignment is refused there too (both added in the step's fix wave). What the web app shows of it: a task whose design review is due shows "Checking the screens" on its page, as its board card does while the Designer's design review session runs, and "Waiting on the Designer" on both while every Designer is paused, the page with a link to resume it, or "The Designer's browser is off" on both while its Playwright connector is off, the page saying so and linking to the Designer's page to turn it on, the active Designer's when a paused one is also on the team (added in the step's fix wave). The task page and the gate of a task whose change is to the interface show the Designer's letter, "<name>, your UI/UX Designer, checked the screens first", with its reasons and whether it passed the change on or sent it back, and "The four checks", one figure per width and theme with the screenshot, the width in pixels, and "No problems found" or each problem's help, rule and impact; on the gate the letter and the checks come before the Architect's review. The letter is signed by the Designer who recorded the review, not the team's first Designer. The Designer's earlier send-backs fold under "<name> sent it back once, on <day>" (or so many times, last on a day), with their reasons. The gate's "About this task" gains "Screens checked", the day of the passing review and its Designer, "Code reviewed", the day of the latest review that passed the code and its reviewer, and "Who looked at it, in order": Catervas's checks, then each design review and code review in time order with its outcome, then "Now you decide" while the work waits on the person. Once the Designer passed the screens, the reviewer's letter is headed "<name>, your <role>, reviewed the code after <Designer> passed the screens", and the send-backs end with "<builder> changed it, and the next look passed." No wire carries what the builder changed in answer to a send-back, so the line does not say it; the builder's letter above it is its latest completion note. `task.get` carries `ui_change` and `design_review`, null for a change that is not to the interface, else its `state` (`not_needed`, `waiting`, `waiting_on_designer`, `preview_missing`, `designer_needs_sandbox`, `designer_needs_browser`, `passed` or `failed`), its `reasons` once recorded, and its `checks`; and `design_reviews`, every `design_review.recorded` of the task oldest first, each with the Designer's `agent_id`, `pass`, `reasons` and `recorded_at`; `tasks.list` gives a task in `verifying` whose change is to the interface its `design_review_state`, the same `state`, which the board's card reads, and leaves it out for a task whose review cannot be read rather than failing the whole list (changed in 0.37); the query `task.screenshot { task_id, file }` answers `{ png_base64 }` for a file one of that task's `page.checked` events names, and `not_found` for any other. A Designer's task page has a side panel, "How <name> works", with the flow's five steps marked done or now and the plans sent back of the number that stops the task.

### 6.9 DevOps Engineer (added in 0.32)

Mandate: deploy what the team integrated, watch production, restore the service when it fails, and fix the cause (ADR 0027; `docs/design/devops-engineer.md`). It is optional: the team builder offers it and does not suggest it. It changes code only in an incident's fix task and in the project's deploy configuration a task names.

Produces: deployments, incident notes, fixes on `fix/CTV-<n>` branches, completion notes.

Cannot: deploy anything but the commit the team integrated; call a platform's write tool itself; change secrets, environment variables, access rights, or scaling, or delete anything in production; open a shell in a production container.

Default tools: read, write_workspace, execute, git_local and network, the Developer's model, and three Catervas tools only it may call, each of the `external_effect` tier and each taking no version or service: `catervas_deploy`, which deploys the commit the default branch holds after the integrations its task depends on; `catervas_restart`, which restarts the service's pods or redeploys the same version where there are none; and `catervas_roll_back`, which returns the service to the last deployment Catervas recorded as healthy. Its reviewer is the Architect, or the Developer when the team has no Architect. Its kit (6.7) connects the one platform the project runs on (AWS ECS or EKS, Kubernetes, Vercel or Netlify, Render, Railway or Fly), with the read tools `network` and every other tool `denied`.

Approval. The three tools are `external_effect` (5.6), and their approval is given in two ways only: the human's start of a sprint approves `catervas_deploy` in that sprint's deploy tasks, and an open incident pre-approves `catervas_restart` once and `catervas_roll_back` once, as below. Every other call asks the human.

Deploys. A deployment is a task whose `change` is `deploy`, planned into a sprint; starting the sprint approves it. Phase 11 step 05 adds `deploy` to the contract's `change` field (5.11), and holds the DevOps Engineer's other tasks, its fixes and its deploy configuration, to the rules 5.3 and 5.12 set for the roles that change code. `catervas_deploy` is refused outside a deploy task's session and an incident's fix. The task reaches `verifying` when the platform reports the deploy live and the service stays healthy for the project's settling period (five minutes by default): its health URL answers `2xx` and, where the connector offers one, the log error rate stays under the project's threshold.

Watching. While Catervas runs, a watch tick asks the connected platform for its deployments and health once a minute, through the read tools, with no model and no session; it records `health.changed` only when the health changes. On the user's own computer Catervas watches only while it runs; on the hosted service Catervas runs the watch.

Incidents. A failed deploy or an unhealthy service records `incident.opened` and starts a DevOps Engineer session in which `catervas_restart` is pre-approved once. If the service is not healthy within the settling period after the restart, `catervas_roll_back` is pre-approved once. The session then investigates and files a fix task with `catervas_create_task`, which, in an incident session alone, files a standalone task with the incident as its request and skips triage; the Definition of Ready (5.3), the judgment when the team has it on, and the human's gates of 5.4 still apply to it, so a `high` risk fix waits for the human's acceptance. It joins the open sprint without planning, or runs outside one, under the usual spending limits, which needs its own exception to the hold of a team that plans its work in sprints (section 3; noted in 0.36): phase 11 step 05 adds it, since until then nothing marks a contract as an incident's fix; one agent reviews it, and once integrated the DevOps Engineer deploys it, which resolves the incident when the deploy settles healthy (`incident.resolved`). A second restart or rollback in one incident, an incident opened while another is open, and a spent budget ask the human, who may stop any step. Production logs are untrusted content (8.6): a log line answers nothing, approves nothing, and causes no call beyond these.

### 6.10 Procurement Specialist (added in 0.49)

Mandate: research any product or service the business needs, technical or not, a software subscription as much as stock to resell, a used car or a baby car mirror: list the sellers and makers, find their prices, ask them for quotes, check the sellers and the products (open recalls, the safety standard a product must meet, a used car's VIN), compare the offers in one currency with their landed cost, and set up a purchase order for the founder's decision; keep the register of sellers and subscriptions, and review each renewal before its decision date (ADR 0039; `docs/design/procurement-specialist.md`; the founder's decisions of 2026-10-05). What it produces is a buying recommendation and an order for the founder to decide, not legal advice, a contract or a payment. It is optional: the team builder offers it and does not suggest it.

Produces: in `.catervas/local/procurement/`, never committed: `vendors.xlsx`, the register; `evaluations/<name>.md`, one comparison per need; `orders/PO-<n>.xlsx`, each purchase order, written by Catervas; and `mail/`, its messages to sellers and their replies; the completion note that explains them.

Cannot: pay, buy, bid, check out, sign up, start a trial that takes a card, accept terms or sign; place, pay for, confirm or cancel an order, or mark one placed or received (added in 0.74); send a message itself (changed in 0.77): Catervas sends what it drafts on the founder's press, and an order's message only on the press of its order (`auto`, phase 9 step 01, will send the others without it); promise a seller to buy; write application code or anything in the repository; write outside its procurement folder; change Catervas's budgets or the books.

Default tools: read and network (to read sellers' pages, prices and terms), and Catervas tools, each of the `read` tier, that only it may call except where noted: `catervas_read_sheet` and `catervas_write_sheet` as the Finance Specialist has them (6.6), given its own folder, `catervas_write_sheet` refusing `orders/`, where Catervas alone writes a purchase order (`orders_are_catervas_s`, the first part of the path compared without regard to case; added in 0.74), and not `catervas_read_costs`, since the team's AI costs stay the Finance Specialist's (the founder's answer of 2026-10-07 to step 10b's readiness review); `catervas_write_evaluation`; `catervas_request_sites` and `catervas_read_sites` (added in 0.71, below); `catervas_draft_purchase_order`, `catervas_read_purchase_orders` and `catervas_update_purchase_order` (added in 0.74, below); `catervas_request_data_pipeline` and `catervas_read_data_pipelines`; `catervas_draft_seller_message`, `catervas_read_seller_messages` and `catervas_read_seller_replies`. Its reviewer is the Product Manager. The Finance Specialist may read `vendors.xlsx` with `catervas_read_sheet`, and nothing else outside the role reads the folder.

As built (added in 0.70; phase 7 step 10b). The role is `procurement_specialist`, "Procurement Specialist", tag "PROC", with the persona "Finds the best seller at the right price", the model `claude-sonnet-5-5` at `medium` effort and the default session limits, tiers exactly `read` and `network`, the Product Manager as reviewer, and no code to change (5.12). A procurement task is reviewed by the Product Manager alone, as a finance task is, and the Finance Specialist never reviews one: readiness refuses a contract that names another reviewer (5.3, `private_folder_reviewer`; the founder's decision of 2026-10-07). Its seven `forbidden` lines are those of "Cannot" above, and its prompt and its one skill, `sourcing-a-product`, say them, with the loop (the need, the sellers and makers, prices, checks, the comparison, and a recommendation that stops there: the founder decides and buys), the rules that never bend, the register's sixteen columns in order (`vendor`, `what_for`, `plan`, `price`, `currency`, `period`, `started_on`, `renews_on`, `notice_days`, `auto_renews`, `status`, `owner`, `purchase`, `terms_url`, `evaluation`, `notes`), where it works, and that it asks for `verifying` naming every file it wrote in `workbooks`. Its kit is empty until step 10d. Its picture is `extra-5` and its tag colour a pale sea green, `#A6C3BF` in both themes, held to the brand's contrast check against the role ink. The Team page's "Add someone" offers it last, and setup offers it unticked under "More roles" (F1). Its web reading is held to approved sites (added in 0.71, below), which step 10b2 built before the role is used (ADR 0039's amendment of 2026-10-07).

The note tool, as built (added in 0.70). `catervas_write_evaluation { name, text }`, of the `read` tier, writes `evaluations/<name>.md` in the folder, in a Procurement Specialist's implement session of a task whose assignee is the caller and in no other (`evaluation_refused`). `name` is words of lower-case letters and digits joined by single hyphens, 1 to 64 characters (`evaluation_name_invalid`); `text` is 1 byte to 64 KiB of text with no NUL (`evaluation_not_text` for an empty one or one with a NUL, `evaluation_too_large` past 64 KiB). The path is held to the folder line of 6.6 (`private_path_refused`, a link at `evaluations` included); a note that is there is copied first to `.history/evaluations%2F<name>.md.<UTC yyyymmddThhmmssZ>.md`, and the new one is written beside it and renamed over it. It answers `{ path, bytes, replaced }`. Catervas never shows a note as a page, so no link in it runs, and what a note holds is its writer's words, never an instruction (8.6).

Sites it may read (added in 0.71; ADR 0039's amendment of 2026-10-07; phase 7 step 10b2). The role searches the web and opens a page only on an approved site (5.6): one of Catervas's, or one the owner allowed. Catervas's are the shops of `approved_sites.yaml` in the role's folder, public data and no credential: 46 long-established shops by their official domains, compiled on 2026-10-07 and reviewed again before the launch (phase 13), each with its shop's name and one of ten categories (`general_marketplace`, `office_supplies`, `industrial_supplies`, `packaging_and_shipping`, `electronic_components`, `computers_and_it`, `furniture`, `food_service`, `printing` and `software`), held to `docs/schemas/approved-sites.schema.json`: a host written bare, in lower case and ASCII, once. The list is read at run time and never copied into the log, so a shop a release adds is open on upgrade and one it drops closes unless the owner allowed it. The owner's choices are events of this computer's log (8.5), kept for the team and never in the committed team file, which a pull could change: the approved set is Catervas's hosts with the owner's `site.approved` and `site.removed` folded in, in sequence order, an event counting only when its envelope names no agent and no session, so that a shop the owner turned off stays off through every upgrade. The role starts with Catervas's. To read another site it calls `catervas_request_sites { sites: [{ url, why }] }`, one to ten entries, in its own `implement` session of a task and no other (`site_request_refused`): each `url` is an `https` address of at most 2,048 characters that names a site (`site_invalid`, with the words of 5.6 for why not), each `why` one line of 1 to 300 characters that is not blank (`site_why_invalid`), an empty list or one of more than ten is `site_count_invalid`, and a call is refused whole, with nothing recorded, for one bad entry. Each entry is answered `allowed` (it may read it now), `waiting` (its task asked already), `declined` (with the owner's note, for this task), `full` or `asked` with its request number, and when any was asked the answer says to end the turn, which 5.7 follows. `catervas_read_sites` answers the sites it may read, Catervas's in the file's order with each shop and category and then the owner's by host, and for its task the hosts waiting and the ones the owner did not allow, with their notes; another role calling it is refused (`sites_refused`). Both are offered in the role's `implement` session of a task, `catervas_read_sites` in its chat, and no other role is offered either. Under `auto` (ADR 0041) a request still waits for the owner: the list is a limit the owner sets, as the spending limits are. The owner decides on Today (F3), adds a site no one asked for with `site_add { site }`, which takes a name such as `shop.com` or the address of any page, keeps its site, turns one of Catervas's back on and allows every request waiting for it, and takes one away with `site_remove { host }`, one of Catervas's or one the owner allowed; each records its event with no task, and `site_invalid`, `site_already_allowed` and `site_not_allowed` refuse what cannot be done. The same is done with `catervas site list`, which prints Catervas's sites by category, with whether each is on and since when, the sites the owner allowed and the requests that wait, and `catervas site approve`, `decline`, `add` and `remove`. The browser reads `sites.list {}`, which answers `{ catervas: [{ host, shop, category, on, at? }], owner: [{ host, at, request? }], waiting: [{ request, host, url, why, task_id, agent_id, at }] }`, and the agent's page shows the first two (F1). The prompt and the skill say which sites the role may read, how it asks for another, and that an address is no place for the business's details.

Its folder. A procurement task follows the finance folder's rules of 6.6 with `.catervas/local/procurement/` in place of `.catervas/local/finance/` (every one keyed by `private_folder(role)`, which gained this arm; none names a role): its session runs in the folder, its `allowed_paths` are under it, it takes no `command` or `test` criterion, it reaches `verifying` without a commit and is finished at `accepted`, and one piece of procurement work touches the folder at a time. The two folders are independent, so a finance task and a procurement task may run at once. Its files are workbooks (`.xlsx`) and notes (`.md`, lower case); a note is checked for being there as a workbook is, is named in `workbooks` and shown to the reviewer as a workbook is, and is searched for no text. The Finance Specialist reads `vendors.xlsx` (6.6).

Purchase orders (rewritten in 0.74; ADR 0039's amendment of 2026-10-07, "suggest, then track"; phase 7 step 10c). The agent suggests an order and tracks it; it never places, pays for, confirms or cancels one, and no Catervas tool records any of those steps or what was paid. An order is drafted (the agent), approved or rejected (the owner), placed (the owner), then received or closed (the owner), or expired (Catervas); from placing to its end it also has a follow-up status, recorded by its own agent from what it learns or by the owner correcting it. Every step but the drafting and a status counts only from an event whose envelope names no agent and no session, as a marketing plan's decision does (8.5), and the store folds an order through its states once, a step counting only from the state it may happen in.

The draft. `catervas_draft_purchase_order { seller, seller_contact, lines, currency, period, delivery, terms, url, evaluation, why }` is a tool of the `read` tier, offered to a Procurement Specialist in the `implement` session of a task it is the assignee of and refused `purchase_order_refused` in any other. It does not end the session, hold the task or ask before running, since nothing outside Catervas happens (5.7). Its input is checked in this order, each refusal named, with nothing written or recorded on any: `seller` 1 to 100 characters on one line with no control character (`purchase_order_seller_invalid`), `seller_contact` 0 to 200 likewise; `lines` 1 to 50 (`purchase_order_lines_invalid`), each `{ item, quantity, unit_price, unit }` with `item` 1 to 200 characters on one line, `quantity` an integer from 1 to 1,000,000, `unit_price` a number with at most eight whole digits and two decimals and no sign or separator, and `unit` 0 to 20 characters (`purchase_order_line_invalid`, with the line and the field); the total, exact in hundredths, at most 10,000,000.00 (`purchase_order_too_large`); `currency` three capital letters (`purchase_order_currency_invalid`); `period` `once`, `month` or `year`, a subscription's lines being per period; `delivery` and `terms` 0 to 600 characters each (`purchase_order_delivery_invalid`, `purchase_order_terms_invalid`); `url` empty or an `https` address that names a site (`purchase_order_url_invalid`, with 5.6's words for why not) which is approved for the team, else `purchase_order_site_not_approved: <host> is not a site the owner allowed; ask for it with catervas_request_sites first, then end your turn` (the founder's answer of 2026-10-07, "Yes, approved sites only"); `evaluation` `evaluations/<name>.md` by the note tool's rule that exists (`purchase_order_evaluation_invalid`, `evaluation_missing`); `why` 20 to 600 characters (`purchase_order_why_invalid`); then no open order for the same seller, without regard to case, on the same task (`purchase_order_open`), an order being open until it is rejected, received, closed or expired; then at most 20 orders waiting for the owner in the project (`too_many_purchase_orders`). An empty `url` is a seller with no page, met by phone or in person.

The number and the workbook. One lock is held from the first read of the orders to the record that changes them, by the draft, by each of the owner's commands, by a status and by the expiry, so that two of them never take one number, decide one order twice or expire an order being placed. The number is one more than the highest among the log's drafted orders and the files `orders/PO-<n>.xlsx`, and is the order everywhere: `PO-<n>`, every command's `order`, the waiting row's, `catervas order approve <n>`. Catervas writes the workbook as a new file in the folder, never replacing one (`purchase_order_file_exists`), then records `purchase_order.drafted`, and removes the file if the log refuses the record; once the log has the order it is recorded and its file stays, whatever the projections do (changed in 0.74 after review). It has one sheet, `Order`: a header block (order number, date, buyer, seller and contact, currency, period, delivery, terms), then a line table (item, quantity, unit, unit price, line total) and the total. Quantities, prices and totals are number cells Catervas computes, never formulas, and text is text, so that the seller and the owner read the same numbers in any program. The buyer is the team's name. The agent never writes `orders/`, and reads the orders with `catervas_read_sheet`.

The owner's steps. Five commands (a sixth, `purchase_order_send`, is under "Contacting sellers"; changed in 0.77), on `POST /command` behind the daemon's token and in the browser's RPC behind its session, each checking and writing under the one lock: `purchase_order_decide { order, decision: approve | reject, note? }` (5.7); `purchase_order_place { order, placed_on?, paid?, currency? }`, the owner placed the order and paid for it themselves; `purchase_order_receive { order, received_on?, paid?, currency?, renews_on? }`; `purchase_order_close { order, note? }`, for a placed order that will not come, one the seller cancelled or refunded or that was lost; and `purchase_order_update { order, status, note?, expected_on? }`, the owner's correction of the follow-up status. The days are ISO days, `placed_on` and `received_on` today (UTC) when absent; `paid` follows `unit_price`'s rule up to 10,000,000.00 (`purchase_order_paid_invalid`) and `currency` three capital letters, the order's when absent beside a `paid` (`purchase_order_currency_invalid`), and a currency with no amount is never recorded; the latest `paid` given, at placing or at receiving, is what was paid; a `note` is at most 600 characters (`purchase_order_note_too_long`). Refusals by state: `unknown_purchase_order` for a number that is no drafted order; `purchase_order_expired` for any step on an order Catervas closed by itself; `purchase_order_decided` for deciding an order not drafted; `purchase_order_not_approved` for placing one not approved and `purchase_order_placed` for placing one placed already; `purchase_order_not_placed` for receiving, closing or correcting one not placed; and `purchase_order_ended` for any step on one received or closed. Under `auto` (ADR 0041) an order still waits: only `purchase_order_decide` from the daemon's token or the browser's session records an approval, and placing, receiving and closing stay the owner's in both positions. The owner does these on the agent's page (F1), which lists every order of the project with the agent that set it up (changed in 0.74 after review), and with `catervas order placed`, `received`, `status` and `close`, which take `<n>` as `12` or `PO-12` and record alone, filing no request.

The follow-up. `catervas_update_purchase_order { order, status, note, expected_on? }` is a `read`-tier tool offered to the Procurement Specialist in the `implement` session of a task it is the assignee of, refused `purchase_order_update_refused` in any other session or role. A status is one of `preparing` ("Being prepared"), `shipped`, `delayed` and `problem` ("A problem": out of stock, a payment refused, cancelled by the seller); `placed`, `received`, `paid` and the rest are `purchase_order_status_invalid`, the agent never saying that the owner placed an order or that it came. A `note` is 0 to 300 characters on one line, 1 to 300 for `delayed` and `problem`; `expected_on` is the day the seller expects the order, ISO and not before the day it is recorded, and `delayed` needs it. An order another agent drafted is `purchase_order_not_yours`, one not placed `purchase_order_not_placed`. It records `purchase_order.updated` with the agent's envelope, so it never counts as the owner's, and the owner's correction, which the owner records with the same body, replaces it while the order is placed. Following up is the agent reading what it may reach, the seller's pages on approved sites; it sends nothing. A follow-up happens in a task: "Mark placed" offers to ask the agent to follow up until the order arrives, ticked, and a placed order has "Ask for a follow-up", each filing the owner's request (`request.file`, 5.16) in words the owner reads and may change first, which the Product Manager triages as any other. A problem that needs the owner reaches them as the agent's question in its task. `catervas_read_purchase_orders {}`, of the `read` tier, offered in the implement session of a task and in the role's chat, gives every order, oldest first, with its state (`drafted`, `approved`, `rejected`, `placed`, `received`, `closed` or `expired`), lines and total, the owner's notes as written (their own words, not wrapped as untrusted, ADR 0011), `placed_on`, what was paid and its currency, `received_on`, `renews_on`, its latest status with who recorded it (`agent` or `owner`), its note and `expected_on`, and `overdue`.

When an order ends. An order not decided within 30 days of its drafting, or approved and not marked placed within 30 days of its approval, closes by itself: on every tick not scoped to a task, before the pause is read, so that it starts no session and a paused team has it too, Catervas records `purchase_order.expired { order }` for it, whatever its task's state, a cancelled task's included. A placed order never closes by itself: the owner has paid, and an order that closed by itself would hide money spent on goods that never came, so it is `overdue` once its expected day (the latest status's `expected_on`, else 30 days after `placed_on`) has passed, and the owner ends it with "Mark received" or "It won't come". The agent's page lists orders to place, placed orders, and the five that ended last (F1).

Contacting sellers (as built in 0.77; phase 7 step 10f). The mailbox. The owner connects one address of their own for buying (a mailbox of its own, or an alias of the main one) with `procurement_mailbox.connect { address, name, provider, imap, smtp, username, password, folder, signature, disclose_ai }`, a method of the browser's RPC behind its session (and `catervas procurement mailbox connect`, which reads the password without echo or from standard input and refuses while another process drives the project), `provider` `gmail`, `icloud`, `fastmail` or `other` (the servers of the first three are Catervas's, those of `other` typed, `host:port`); `microsoft` is refused `mailbox_provider_unsupported` before anything is read, as is an address at Outlook.com, Hotmail, Live or MSN, since Microsoft no longer lets a mail program sign in with a password. Connecting logs in to both servers over TLS (the platform's certificates, always checked; a server that does not offer TLS is refused `mailbox_needs_tls`, an untrusted certificate `mailbox_certificate`, an unreachable server `mailbox_unreachable`, a refused login `mailbox_login_failed`), selects the folder read-only to read its `UIDVALIDITY` and `UIDNEXT`, sends nothing, keeps the password as a `Secret` (whose `Debug` prints `[redacted]`) in the keychain under `mailbox:<project id>:procurement` or, on a computer with none, in `connectors.json`, writes `mail/mailbox.json` and `mail/ledger.json` and records `mailbox.connected`. `procurement_mailbox.disconnect` forgets the password and deletes `mail/mailbox.json`, keeps `mail/out/` and `mail/in/`, and records `mailbox.disconnected`; `procurement_mailbox.check` reads now; the query `procurement_mailbox.get` answers `{ connected, address?, name?, provider?, folder?, signature?, disclose_ai?, checked_at?, error?, restarted_at?, sent_today, cap }`. Drafting. The agent writes to a seller or a maker only by `catervas_draft_seller_message { seller, to, subject, body, purpose, purchase_order? }`, a `read`-tier tool offered to the Procurement Specialist in the `implement` session of a task it is the assignee of that has a contract and in no other (refused `seller_message_refused`), with `seller` 1 to 100 characters, `to` one address with no display name and no list, at most 254 characters, on a domain `site_of` accepts and not the procurement mailbox's own, `subject` 1 to 200 characters on one line and `body` 1 to 8000 characters of plain text; `purpose` `quote_request`, `question` or `purchase_order`, the last requiring `purchase_order`, one drafted order of the caller that has no message yet, `question` allowing one the owner placed, and `quote_request` refusing it. At most 20 messages wait in a project. It writes `mail/out/<n>.txt` and records `seller_message.drafted { message, seller, to, subject, purpose, purchase_order?, sha256 }`, sending nothing; the message's number is one more than the highest among the log's and the files' in `mail/out/`, taken under the mail lock and counted from 1 (changed in 0.77 after review). The tool refuses with Catervas's words (`seller_message_field_invalid`, `seller_message_too_long`, `seller_address_invalid`, `seller_message_order_invalid`, `seller_order_message_waiting` for an order that has a message already, and `seller_drafts_full`). Sending. Only the owner sends: `seller_message_send { message, subject, body }` (`catervas procurement send <n>`, which prints the message whole first, or Today's "Send" or the dialog's after "Edit") adds the owner's signature and, unless turned off, "Written with an AI assistant and sent by <name> after reading it.", sends it from the procurement mailbox over SMTP and records `seller_message.sent { message, message_id, sha256, edited }`, keeping what went out whole as `mail/out/<n>.sent.txt`; `edited` says the owner changed the subject or the words, and the agent reads what was sent, not its draft. A server that refuses the message records `seller_message.failed { message, why }` with Catervas's sentence and the message waits, tried again by the owner's next press. `seller_message_discard { message }` records `seller_message.discarded`. At most 50 messages go in a UTC day, an order's included (`seller_send_limit`). Refusals: `unknown_seller_message`, `seller_message_sent`, `seller_message_discarded`, `seller_message_closed` (an order's message whose order was decided without it; added in 0.77 after review), `seller_message_is_an_order` (an order's message goes with its order), `mailbox_not_connected`, `seller_message_failed`. A send claims its message in memory, holds no lock while the servers answer, and records after; a claim dropped by a command cut off gives the message back. Approve and send. For an order whose message was drafted (`purpose` `purchase_order`), `purchase_order_send { order, message, subject, body, note? }` (the sixth owner's command of the orders, in the browser as "Approve and send to <seller>") emails the message with the order's workbook, `orders/PO-<n>.xlsx`, attached, to the seller and only then records `purchase_order.approved` (with the note), `seller_message.sent` and `purchase_order.placed` (today, UTC), each with no agent and no session; a failed send records `seller_message.failed` alone and the order still waits, and a second "Approve and send" of an order whose email is on its way is refused `seller_message_sent` ("This message is being sent."), while `purchase_order_decide` on an order being emailed is refused `purchase_order_sending` (changed in 0.77 after review). An order's message is sent by this command alone, never by `seller_message_send`, and the owner pays the seller outside Catervas: Catervas never pays. Replies. Every 15 minutes while a process drives the project, on a tick not scoped to a task and before the pause is read, so that a paused team reads mail too, with no model and no session, and on `procurement_mailbox.check`, at most one check at a time, Catervas reads the mailbox. It selects the folder read-only and fetches with `BODY.PEEK`, so it marks nothing read, moves nothing and deletes nothing, and it opens a message only when it is for the procurement address (named in To, Cc or Delivered-To), answers a Message-ID Catervas sent or comes from an address Catervas wrote to, and is at most 25 MB. An alias that shares the user's whole mailbox therefore shows Catervas only what the rule lets through. Each such message is kept under `mail/in/<yyyy-mm>/<n>/`, the month of the check that read it, which also stamps its record (changed in 0.77 after review): `text.txt` (plain text, an HTML-only message with its markup removed, at most 64 KiB) and the attachments that are PDFs or pictures of at most 10 MB, numbered from 1 and named by their number (`1.pdf`); any other file is listed by the name the sender gave it and not kept, and at most 100 attachments are listed, the first ones; a message with no sender is listed as "unknown sender" (changed in 0.77 after review). It records `seller_reply.received { reply, message, from, subject, date?, attachments: [{ name, kept, media_type?, bytes }] }` for the message of Catervas's it answers (by `In-Reply-To` and `References`, else the latest message to that sender). If the provider renumbers the folder (`UIDVALIDITY` changes) Catervas starts again after the last message and says so in `restarted_at`. A check that cannot read the mailbox keeps Catervas's sentence in `error`. A reply that cannot be kept or recorded ends the check there (changed in 0.77 after review): the replies before it are recorded once and passed, the ledger stays just before it with the time of the check and Catervas's own sentence in `error` ("Catervas could not keep a reply from the mailbox; it tries again in 15 minutes.", never the store's words, which repeat the seller's), the refusal is `mailbox_files`, and the next check, 15 minutes later, tries it again. Queries `seller_messages.list` and `seller_replies.list` answer the messages and replies with the seller and the subject Catervas sent (the subject shown for a reply is never the reply's own); a message that waits after a try that failed carries `why` and `failed_at`, the time of the latest failure the mailbox recorded (added in 0.77 after review); `seller_reply.attachment { reply, index }` answers a kept file as base64; `seller_reply_dismiss { reply }` records `seller_reply.dismissed`, and the reply stays kept. What the agent reads. `catervas_read_seller_messages` lists every message of the project with its state (`waiting`, `sent`, `discarded` or `closed`), why the last try failed, whether the owner edited it and its text (what was sent, for a sent one); `catervas_read_seller_replies { message? }` lists the replies, each reply's sender, subject, date, text and file names inside the untrusted-content notice of 8.6 (at most 80 KiB), and the paths of the files Catervas kept, which `Read` opens. Both are `read`-tier tools offered to the Procurement Specialist in the `implement` session of its own task (one it is the assignee of) and in its chat, and refused `seller_mail_refused` anywhere else, another Procurement Specialist's task included (changed in 0.77 after review). No tool of an agent sends, discards or records a send, a failure, a reply or a connection, and the log counts those only from an event whose envelope names no agent and no session. The command line: `catervas procurement mailbox connect|disconnect|show`, `check`, `messages`, `send <n>` and `discard <n>`; `send` and `discard` are commands sent to the process driving the project, and an order's message (`purpose` `purchase_order`) is sent from the browser alone, while a follow-up question, which names an order but is no order's message, is sent from the command line like any other (changed in 0.77 after review). Today shows each message that waits, whole, and each reply not dismissed, and the agent's page the mailbox (F1, F3). The user's main mailbox is never read beyond the alias rule, for 6.6's reasons. Under `auto` (ADR 0041; phase 9 step 01) a message will go as drafted within the daily cap; until then every message goes on the owner's press.

Data pipelines (the founder's decisions of 2026-10-05; rewritten in 0.76, phase 7 step 10e). When a source of prices or seller data it lacks would change a recommendation, the agent asks with `catervas_request_data_pipeline { name, what, source_url, why, cost, needs_account, sends_project_data }`, a tool of the `read` tier offered to a Procurement Specialist in the `implement` session of a task it is the assignee of and refused `pipeline_refused` in any other (`catervas_read_data_pipelines`, which gives its requests with their states, the Product Manager's reasons inside the untrusted notice, is also in its chat). `cost` is `free`, `paid` or `unknown`; `name` is one line of 1 to 100 characters, `what` and `why` 20 to 600 and `source_url` an `https` address of at most 2000 characters (changed in 0.76 after review) with no credential in it (`pipeline_field_invalid`, `pipeline_url_invalid`); three requests may be open at once (`pipeline_limit_reached`), and a name asked for twice, compared without regard to case, is refused while the first is open or approved (`pipeline_already_requested`). The tool records `data_pipeline.requested` on the task and holds no task: the session goes on, the task keeps its status, and a `source_url` on a site not approved is accepted and approves no site (5.6). The decision is a rule of the tick, not a task's session. An unscoped tick with every rule on and an open request starts one `verify` session of the active Product Manager, about no task and counted against none (its dollars count against the day's, 5.5), whose one Catervas tool is `catervas_decide_data_pipeline { pipeline, decision, reason }` and no built-in tool: `approve`, `decline` or `escalate`, with a reason of 20 to 600 characters on one line. Its message holds every field of the request inside the untrusted notice (8.6). Its `session.started` names the request in `pipeline`. With no active Product Manager, a spent day or a sleeping manager the request waits. Only that session decides (`pipeline_decision_refused`), once (`pipeline_decided`), and a number that is no request is `unknown_pipeline`. When three such sessions have ended and the request is still open, Catervas records `data_pipeline.escalated` itself with the reason "The Product Manager did not decide" and no agent or session. The Product Manager judges whether the pipeline is needed; the governor refuses its `approve` (`pipeline_needs_owner`) for one whose `cost` is `paid` or `unknown`, or that has `sends_project_data`, since the project's data would leave the machine; such a request the Product Manager can still decline or escalate, and an escalated one waits for the owner (5.7), who approves or declines it on Today or with `catervas pipeline approve <n>` and `catervas pipeline decline <n>`. A free source that needs an account may be approved by the Product Manager. `data_pipeline.approved` files an ordinary request (5.16) for the team to set the source up, titled "Set up <name> for the Procurement Specialist.", `created_by` the Product Manager's id when it approved and `human` when the owner did, whose text holds the source's name, what it gives, its address and why, and, when the name is a connector of the role's kit, "Connect <title> on the Procurement Specialist's page."; approval connects, pays for and builds nothing, and approves no site. A decision names no task. The skills `requesting-a-data-pipeline` (the Procurement Specialist's) and `deciding-data-pipelines` (the Product Manager's) carry the judgment. This step builds no `auto` mode: under ADR 0041's `auto` (phase 9 step 01) the Product Manager may approve a `paid` or `unknown` source itself, recorded `by: auto`, but one that sends the project's data out is refused in both modes and a request it escalates or did not decide still waits for the owner.

Renewals (rewritten in 0.74; phase 7 step 10c). While a process drives the project and the team has an active Procurement Specialist, a tick with no model and no session reads the `Vendors` sheet of `vendors.xlsx` once a UTC day and records `renewal.flagged { vendor, renews_on, decide_by }` for a row that is due, and `renewal.checked { due, unreadable }` for each day's run, which is how the next tick knows the day's check ran. The columns are found by their heading in the sheet's first row, `vendor`, `renews_on`, `notice_days` and `status`, case and surrounding spaces aside, so a column the user moved still reads; a date cell is read as its day. A row is read only when its `status` is `active` or `trial`; any other is neither due nor counted. The decision date is `renews_on` less `notice_days` (blank is 0); a row is due when today (UTC) is from 14 days before its decision date to `renews_on`, inclusive, and no `renewal.flagged` exists for that vendor, without regard to case, and that `renews_on`. A blank `renews_on` is no renewal; a `renews_on` that is not an ISO date, a `notice_days` neither blank nor an integer from 0 to 365, and a `vendor` blank, over 100 characters or holding a line break or control character are each counted and never guessed. A workbook with no `Vendors` sheet, or whose first row lacks any of the four headings, counts as one row it cannot read, or as many as the rows below the first that hold something; a register Catervas cannot open, or one past 10 MiB, counts as one row it cannot read and never stops the team, and one that is a link is not read at all, as the folder's other files are not. Today lists the renewals open, oldest first: `renewals.list {}` answers `{ open: [{ renewal, vendor, renews_on, decide_by, flagged_at }], unreadable }`, `renewal` the sequence number of its `renewal.flagged`, `open` every one with no `renewal.dismissed` the owner recorded, and `unreadable` the last `renewal.checked`'s (0 before any); a renewal is not a `waiting.list` row, which needs a task. "Ask for a review" opens the request "Review <vendor> before it renews on <renews_on>; decide by <decide_by>." in a field the owner reads and may change, and "Send to the team" files it (`request.file`, 5.16) and then dismisses the renewal, a refused request dismissing nothing; "Dismiss" records `renewal.dismissed` at once, and the register is unchanged. The owner's `renewal_dismiss { renewal }` is refused `unknown_renewal` for a number that is no `renewal.flagged` and `renewal_dismissed` for a second. `catervas renewal list` prints the open renewals and how many rows it could not read, and `catervas renewal dismiss <n>` dismisses one.

Kit (6.7; built in 0.75 to 0.78, phase 7 steps 10d to 10g): fourteen skills and seven services, none of which can buy. The services: Catervas's own `fx` (exchange rates over Frankfurter), `recalls` (US product and vehicle recalls, complaints, crash ratings and VIN decoding, over the CPSC and NHTSA, with no account) and `ebay` (eBay's listing search over its official API, with the user's own free developer keys); Exa (web search, no account), SerpApi (shopping prices across Google Shopping, Amazon and Walmart, its `search` `external_effect` with an allowance, since each search spends the user's); Brex (what the company already spends with a seller, signed in, read-only); and AWS Pricing (for a software team, a key that may only read prices). Nothing in the kit can buy. eBay is read only through `ebay` (the founder, 2026-10-07, asked whether to follow eBay's rules: "Follow eBay's rules"): eBay's User Agreement (posted 2026-01-20, in force 2026-02-20) bars "LLM-driven bots… to access our Services for any purpose, except with the prior express permission of eBay", so the skills read eBay never through SerpApi's `ebay` engine and never by opening ebay.com pages, and say eBay was not checked when `ebay` is not connected. `ebay`'s setup asks the user, before the first production call, to declare "Not persisting eBay data" (eBay's choice about a marketplace account's deletion), since Catervas never keeps an eBay member's name, and its answers carry no seller's username, which eBay's API License Agreement forbids collecting, only the seller's feedback score and percentage and the listing's address. Catervas's approved sites keep `ebay.com` for now, since step 10c's purchase-order address check uses it; the review of the list in phase 13 step 01 decides it. Exa's search can show the agent text from any site's pages, not only the approved ones (the founder, 2026-10-07: "Keep it, say so"), so Exa's setup says it: the agent still opens a page only on an approved site and sends the other sites nothing, and what it searches for, with its one-sentence objective, goes to Exa as written. SerpApi's lens and reverse-image engines open an address on SerpApi's own servers, so its setup says that Catervas lets the agent give it only addresses on sites the owner approved, but that SerpApi may follow a link from there to another site (the founder, 2026-10-08: "Keep it, say so"; 8.6; added in 0.75 after review). Every seller's page, price, listing and reply is untrusted content (8.6).

## 7. Functional requirements

Numbered so the milestone plan and tests can refer to them.

**F1 Team builder.** Create, edit, pause, retire agents. Assign roles. Set names, avatars (from a shipped pixel set or an uploaded 32x32 image), and persona lines. Suggest the six of 4.1 at setup, and offer the UI/UX Designer among the roles of an agent added later (added in 0.31). Offer the Finance Specialist there too, and never suggest it at setup; an agent added as one takes the picture `finance-specialist` and a spare name, and every other added agent keeps the extras (added in 0.54). Offer the Procurement Specialist there too, last, and never suggest it at setup; an agent added as one takes the picture `extra-5`, which leaves the extras `extra-2` and `extra-3`, shared again once both are taken, to every other added agent (added in 0.70; the extras were three until then). Setup's team step lists the two roles under the suggested six and "Add someone", under the heading "More roles" and the line "Roles Catervas does not suggest.
